<meta>This page serves as the title page for a collection of alchemical and philosophical treatises compiled by William Cooper (W. C.). It establishes the themes of the book: the transmutation of metals, the nature of life, and the spiritual "light" of wisdom.</meta>

# THE
# Philosophical Epitaph of *W. C. Esquire*.
->For a *Remember Death* <note>original: Memento Mori</note> on his Tombstone.<-

With three hieroglyphical shields <note>original: Scutcheons; heraldic shields containing symbolic imagery</note>, and their philosophical mottoes and explanation; with the philosophical *mercury*, the *nature* of *seed* and *life*, and the *growth* of *metals*; and a discovery of the immortal liquid *Alkahest* <note>original: Al-chahest; a hypothetical universal solvent sought by alchemists</note>. The salt of *tartar* volatilized, and other *elixirs*, with their differences.

->A L S O,<-

## A Brief Account of the Golden Calf (the World's Idol).
Discovering the rarest miracle in nature: how, by the smallest proportion of the Philosopher's Stone, a large piece of common lead was totally transmuted into the purest, transparent gold at the *Hague* in 1666 by *Johann Friedrich Helvetius*.

->A N D,<-

## The *GOLDEN ASS* Well Managed, and *Midas* Restored to Reason; Or, *A New Chemical Light*,
Demonstrating to the blind world that good gold may be found in cold regions as well as hot, and can be profitably extracted out of sand, stones, gravel, and flints, etc., to be worked by all sorts of people. Written by *Johann Rudolf Glauber*.

->W I T H<-

# JEHIOR <note>original: Jehior; a Hebrew term often translated as "Let there be light"</note>. [*The Dawn of Wisdom* <note>original: Aurora Sapientiæ</note>], Or, the Day-Dawning or Light of Wisdom, containing the three principles or origin of all things; whereby the great and many mysteries in God, Nature, and the Elements—hitherto hidden—are now revealed.

## All Published by *W. C. Esquire* <note>William Cooper, a famous London bookseller and specialist in alchemical literature</note>.
## With a Catalogue of *Chemical Books*

---

*London*, Printed by *T. R.* and *N. T.* for *William Cooper*, at the *Pelican* in *Little Britain*, in the year of our Lord <note>original: Anno Dom</note> 167<unclear>3</unclear>

### For a *Remember Death* <note>original: Memento Mori</note> on his Tombstone.

## A Brief Account of the Golden Calf (the World's Idol).
Discovering the rarest miracle in nature: how, by the smallest portion of the Philosopher's Stone, a large piece of common lead was totally transmuted into the purest, radiant gold at the Hague in 1666, by *Johann Friedrich Helvetius*.

->AND,<-

Illustrating what kind of "calf with ears" <note>An archaic way of referring to a fool or a person lacking wisdom</note> is <unclear>kept</unclear> at home; it being a brief word showing that good <unclear>gold</unclear> is better than... in <unclear>cold</unclear> <unclear>silver</unclear> for a <unclear>great</unclear> good case; described by <unclear>Johann Rudolf Glauber</unclear>.

## To the Honourable
# ROBERT BOYLE, Esq;
### Eminently Noble and Accomplished.

*Honourable Sir,*

The translation of *Helvetius's* <note>original: Helvetius his</note> *Golden Calf* included here was licensed and registered for printing in *September 1668*. Since then, another person took the opportunity to print and publish the same work, with very little difference. So that, without unfairness to the translator, we might say with *Virgil*,
->I wrote these small verses, etc. <note>original: *Hos ego versiculos, &c*; a reference to a famous anecdote where the poet Virgil complained, "I wrote these verses, but another took the credit" (*Hos ego versiculos feci, tulit alter honores*).</note><-

<meta>This page begins the author's preface (the "Epistle") to the reader, following the dedication to Elias Ashmole. The author explains his reasons for publishing this work, which he originally intended to be posthumous.</meta>

<image-desc>Two horizontal rows of repeating woodcut ornamental fleurons or urn-like decorative motifs.</image-desc>

### THE
# AUTHOR’S EPISTLE
## To the Courteous and Well-minded READER.

Reader,

I did not think of publishing this, my epitaph, or these symbolically figured shields <note>original: "Hyeroglyphical Figur’d Scutcheons"; heraldic shields or emblems containing hidden alchemical meanings.</note> beyond my gravestone. I was in a "living grave" <note>This likely refers to a period of severe illness or perhaps imprisonment where the author expected to die.</note> and in despair of life when I created them. However, since *Almighty God* has graciously extended the *thread* of my *life* and providentially set these accompanying *treatises* as my task before I died—and having been earnestly urged by a *friend* to publish and explain them—I thought it right to offer my humble contribution <note>original: "mean Mite"</note> to the *world*.

In this way, you may not only see and read a riddle <note>original: "Ænygma"</note> in these *shields* and my *epitaph*, but also have me as your Oedipus <note>original: "Ædypus"; the Greek mythological figure who solved the riddle of the Sphinx. Here, it means a riddle-solver or interpreter.</note> to explain them. Here I have also described the *Philosopher’s Stone* and shown the causes and methods for the *multiplication* of *life* and *seed*. As a bonus, I have provided a clear taste of the *Alcahest* <note>original: "Alchahest"; a hypothesized universal solvent sought by alchemists.</note> and *volatilized salt of tartar* <note>Potassium carbonate treated to become vaporous; a substance highly valued in chemical medicine.</note>, along with other *elixirs* and *philosophical medicines*, etc., in five small, concise *chapters*. These are intended to help you move one step forward in this knowledge, whether you require my help or if it is beyond my ability to shape your

<meta>catchword: back</meta>

<summary>In this preface, William Cooper explains that he initially wrote these alchemical riddles while expecting to die, but having recovered, he is now publishing them along with explanations and chapters on alchemical secrets like the Philosopher's Stone and the Alcahest.</summary>
<keywords>William Cooper, Alcahest, Philosopher's Stone, Salt of Tartar, alchemical emblems, epitaph, Oedipus, riddle-solving, 17th-century chemistry</keywords>

--- Page 22 ---
<meta>This page continues and concludes the author’s epistle to the reader. The author is finishing his explanation of why he is publishing these works and what the reader can expect to find in the subsequent sections, including references to famous alchemical texts by Helvetius and Glauber.</meta>

...reluctance to put your *talent* to use <note>original: "Usury"; here meaning to put to use for interest or profit, rather than exploitative lending.</note> and for the profit of your neighbor. I wish all men would study *substance* and matter with *Laconian brevity* <note>original: "Laconick-Brevity"; a concise, blunt way of speaking named after the Spartans of Laconia.</note> and plainness in their writings, rather than long-winded, *puffed-up eloquence* and *ostentation*. In this way, our *life* might be improved in sound *knowledge* and *virtue*, and *God* may receive all *praise* and *honor*, to whom it is *eternally* due. 

Now, to this short *epitaph* of mine and its *explanation*, I have added *Pythagoras’s metaphysical philosophical figure*. I have also attached *abbreviated* notes on *Helvetius’s Golden Calf* and *Glauber’s New Chemical Light*, which *treat* the rarest *transmutation* and *miracles* of *nature*. Likewise, I include unheard-of *extractions* of *gold* and *silver* (*and something even better*) from all types of *sands* and from the very *stones* of the *streets*, for the *relief* of all people. 

*Written from a true desire to be*

<margin>
January 10,
1670.
</margin>

->*Your Christian Friend and Servant,*<-

->*W. C. For twice five hundred.* <note>W.C. stands for the author, William Cooper. "Twice five hundred" equals 1,000, the Roman numeral M.</note><-

->*Friend, you choose the gold of the field.* <note>original Latin: "L' Aurum Amice eligis Rus." This phrase is an anagram for the Latinized name of the author, "Guilelmus Cooperus" (William Cooper).</note><-

<summary>William Cooper concludes his preface by advocating for plain, useful writing over vanity and introduces the appended alchemical texts by Helvetius and Glauber regarding the extraction of precious metals.</summary>

## CHAPTER 1.

### A clear and full explanation of the afore-
### said Epitaph, coats of arms <note>original: "scutcheons"</note>, and mottos
### of W. C., pertaining to both the Philoso-
### pher's Stone and his own gravestone.

<image-desc>An ornamental woodcut drop cap for the letter 'T' featuring a stylized vine and leaf motif.</image-desc>

This epitaph is literally the work of philosophers, yet it may also bring back to mind the old and useful adage and motto on this author's tombstone: "Remember your end" <note>original: ♄, the symbol for Saturn, who represents time, lead, and mortality</note>. For just as this flourishing Mercury <note>original: ☿, the symbol for the planet and metal Mercury</note> signifies that this author, W.C., is a "Mercurialist" <term>an alchemist or one devoted to the study of the element Mercury</term> who is tired of all worldly pollutions <note>original: "inquinaments"</note>, it also illustrates all the planets and their "mercury" (their essential spirit), the universal spirit and life-force <note>original: "mercury"</note> of the world, and the specific elements of nature. Furthermore, it represents the true "Mercury of the Philosophers" required for this "Great Work": a substance free from all filthy corruptions, properly prepared, and placed "naked"—without any foreign matter <note>original: "Garib," an Arabic-derived alchemical term meaning "stranger" or "impure additive"</note> or anything strange—into its glass container, its private philosophical "nest" or vessel (serving as both a grave and a coffin). There, it is kept under constant, steamy heat for decay <note>original: "putrifaction"</note> and its true preparation, purification, and perfection. This process moves through an orderly progression of colors until it becomes the true "Sulphur of the Philosophers," which, in the meantime, fulfills that philosophical saying:

<meta>catchword: Is <note>original: Est</note></meta>

# CHAPTER 1.

*A plain and full explanation of the aforesaid Epitaph, Shields, and Mottoes of W. C., as well for the Philosopher’s Stone as for his own tombstone.*

<image-desc>A large decorative woodcut initial T with foliate and scrolling vine patterns.</image-desc>

This epitaph is literally the work of Philosophers, and yet it may revive the old useful adage <note>original: "Adagy"</note> and motto upon this author's tombstone: "Remember <note>The author uses the planetary symbol for Jupiter (♃) as a personal monogram or signature.</note> your end." For just as this flourishing symbol <note>original: ♃</note> signifies that this author, W. C., being an alchemist <note>original: "Mercurialist"</note>, is tired of all worldly pollutions <note>original: "Inquinaments"</note>, it also illustrates all the planets and their Mercury, the universal spirit and Mercury of the world, and the specific elements <note>original: "specificks"</note> of Nature. 

It represents no less the true "Mercury of the Philosophers" for this work: free from all filthy corruptions, well-fitted, and placed naked without any foreign substance <term>Garib: an Arabic-derived alchemical term meaning "strange" or "extrinsic" matter</term> or any strange thing into its glass and private "philosophical nest" or vessel (as if into a grave and coffin). There it stays with constant, vaporous heat for decomposition <term>putrefaction: the essential first stage of the alchemical process where the matter decays and turns black before it can be purified</term>, and for its true preparation, purification <note>original: "rectification"</note>, and perfection. This proceeds orderly through its progression of colors until it becomes the true "Sulfur of the Philosophers," which, in the meantime, fulfills that philosophical saying:

<meta>catchword: is (translated from "Est")</meta>

<summary>This page introduces an alchemical interpretation of the author's personal epitaph and symbols, equating the chemical process of "putrefaction" in a vessel to a body in a coffin. It establishes "W. C." as an alchemist seeking to purify both his soul and his chemical materials.</summary>

<keywords>W. C., William Cooper, Philosopher's Stone, Mercury, Putrefaction, Sulfur of the Philosophers, Alchemy, Jupiter symbol, Memento Mori</keywords>

--- Page 31 ---
<meta>This page continues the alchemical and spiritual explanation of the author's epitaph, focusing on the symbolic "Mercury" and the stages of the alchemical process—moving from the base elements to the creation of the Elixir.</meta>

> *The path is toward Heaven, but the heavy Air hinders me,*
> *And moisture has drenched me, which quickly leaves me.*
> *For this, I have tears, but there is no cause for grief, etc.*
> <note>original Latin: "Est iter ad Cœlum, sed me gravis impedit Aer, / Et me perfudit, qui me cito deserit humor. / Huic mihi sunt Lachrymæ, sed non est causa doloris, &c."</note>

->*Translated into English as follows:*<-

> *It tends toward Heaven, but the dense <note>original: "gross"</note> Air hinders it,*
> *And fallen moisture quickly turns to ashes <note>original: "Cinders"</note>.*
> *From this come these tears, though there is no cause for grief,*
> *For they only nourish; the Earth gave them relief.*
> *And though worms feed upon my carcass here,*
> *My soul is in Heaven with my dear Savior.*

In this way, you see it may appear double, or as "one in two"—Male and Female, Superior and Inferior, Dense and Subtle, Celestial and Terrestrial, Sulfur and Mercury, Water and Earth, Corruptible and Incorruptible (or Spiritual). Likewise, the parts are also three: Body, Soul, and Spirit; Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury; Gold (☉), Silver (☽), and Mercury (☿); Lime <term>Calx: a powdery substance produced by roasting or burning a mineral, representing the "body" in alchemy</term>, Ferment, and Tincture. Even Mercury itself may be called threefold: preparing, prepared, and essential; or according to Ripley <note>Sir George Ripley (c. 1415–1490), a famous English alchemist</note> and Raymond <note>Raymond Lully (c. 1232–1315), a major medieval philosopher to whom many alchemical works were attributed</note>, it is calcining, reviving, and essential.

In the same way, it may be termed fourfold; for the Water and Earth, which are the two visible elements, contain Fire and Air. These are the four elements, which are turned "inside outward," thereby showing their effects and properties. Thus: *Earth stands firm; water washes, fire purges, and the spirit enters.* <note>original Latin/Greek: "Terra Stat; unda Lavat, pyr Purgat, Spiritus intrat."</note>

In and for this process, four fires are also used: Natural, Against Nature, Unnatural <note>original: "Innatural"</note>, and Elemental. All of these will eventually produce a "fifth essence" <term>quintessence: the purest, most concentrated medicinal force, believed to be the fifth element beyond earth, air, fire, and water</term>. So, by a perfect process of three <term>Ternary</term>, four <term>Quadrate</term>, and five <term>Quintessential</term>—moving from one, two, three, four, and five—it returns again into one most perfect spiritual substance. It is then reunited and raised to a perfect "Circular Center," a stable <term>fixed: resistant to change or evaporation by heat</term>, meltable <note>original: "fusible"</note>, and incorruptible medicine to create the true "Elixir of the Philosophers," opening and shut-

<meta>catchword: ting</meta>

<summary>This page explains the symbolic and numerical structure of the alchemical process, describing how the "Mercury" of the philosophers moves through stages of three, four, and five to become the unified Elixir.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, Elixir of the Philosophers, Mercury, Sulfur, Salt, four elements, quintessence, George Ripley, Raymond Lully, spiritual transformation</keywords>

--- Page 32 ---
<meta>This page continues the alchemical discussion from the previous page, specifically concluding a sentence about the elements and transitioning into the symbolic meaning of the author's name and the core stages of the alchemical process.</meta>

<page-num>3</page-num>
<sig>B 2</sig>

distributing at pleasure, giving the keys of happiness to all who shall enjoy it, to enter a kingdom of health, wealth, and honor, and shutting out all ignorant, dark bodies and spirits. Thus, at last, this medicine may obtain the name and number suggested by *W. C.* Just as this is the author’s name—who is but one person, yet in figures represents twice five hundred <note>W can be seen as "VV" (5+5=10) and C is 100 in Roman numerals; 10 times 100 is 1,000, or "twice five hundred"</note>—so the medicine is but one in substance, yet in virtue it is twice five hundred, or a thousand. For this reason, the *Jews* thought *Christ* was *John the Baptist* risen from the dead, and therefore performed such mighty works. *And this we know (says St. Paul) that as he is, so shall we be at the Resurrection, if we have his Spirit and follow him in pious obedience, patience, and humility.*

So in this epitaph, as well as by the mentioned shields <note>original: "scutcheons"</note> and mottoes, the divine and natural Stone of the Wise Men <note>The Philosopher's Stone</note> is plainly set forth, along with its sulfur and mercury—though this should be understood with a grain of salt—as well as the moral, natural, and mortal state of man.

The whole art of this philosophy, therefore, is to begin where Nature ends: to take what you find most ready <margin>13</margin> and perfect in Nature, and that which is most closely related to it, and entirely separate the <term>heterogeneous</term> <note>original: "Heterogeneal"; meaning diverse or unrelated parts</note> coarse parts, and gather the <term>homogeneous</term> <note>original: "Homogeneal"; meaning similar or unified parts</note> ones. Make them <term>essential</term>, separate the <term>elements</term>, kill the <term>living</term> <note>original: "Quick"</note>, and bring life to the <term>dead</term>. Then <term>circulate</term>, <term>fix</term>, and <term>ferment</term> everything to the highest degree of <term>exaltation</term>, <term>philosophical sublimation</term>, and <term>perfection</term>.

As *Ripley* <note>Sir George Ripley, a famous 15th-century English alchemist</note> says: "Kill the living, and give life to the dead; make the Trinity one without any strife." Thus, opening and shutting by <term>Ixion’s Wheel</term> <note>A symbol for the circular alchemical process of distillation and condensation</note> in a heavenly dwelling, both in a natural and an artificial vessel, until it reaches the greatest perfection and number—if not infinity. And now note: although most philosophers in their writings have concealed their true, private <term>mercury</term>, <term>fire</term>, <term>vessel</term>, <term>time</term>, and <term>bath</term>, here you may easily find the entire secret—if God has ordained you to be helpful...

<summary>The author explains how his name and the alchemical "Medicine" share a symbolic value of one thousand and outlines the essential alchemical process of purifying elements to reach perfection.</summary>
<keywords>W.C., alchemy, Philosopher's Stone, George Ripley, Ixion's Wheel, Resurrection, sulfur, mercury, sublimation</keywords>

--- Page 33 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the alchemical medicine, or the Philosopher's Stone, explaining how its preparation mirrors the divine process of redemption and the various philosophical interpretations of its composition.</meta>

<page-num>4</page-num>

...towards the redemption of his poor creatures, groaning under their burdens of oppression and mortality.

Now, just as this epitaph sets forth the true Elixir of Philosophers and man’s mortality, so too do these shields <note>original: "scutcheons"; heraldic symbols or emblems</note> or hieroglyphic figures you see. They illustrate the honorable lineage of the true philosophical medicine, or "Golden Fleece," which benefits the life and health of both the human body and metals. This process involves the elements and principles of the Elixir in its celestial and terrestrial parts, proceeding from their "saline chaos" (or first mercurial matter) and their glorified sulfur, through to their celestial spheres of multiplication, fermentation, and projection. 

Thus, they and their mottoes agree perfectly with all philosophical sayings and intentions. For example, some philosophers would have it be one single thing, affirming that the salt of metals is the Philosopher’s Stone. Others say that everything the wise seek is found in mercury. Again, others teach that the whole art depends upon the true preparation of their sulfur, as it is the most perfect of the three principles; its orbits must be turned around three times, as shown in my three figures and celestial wheels. 

Some would have it be one thing that contains the nature of two, like a hermaphrodite or an embryo. Moreover, some would have it be strictly two things, such as male and female, fire and water, water and earth, sulfur and mercury, or heaven and earth. Some likewise would have it consist of three: salt, sulfur, and mercury—represented by the Sun ☉, Moon ☽, and Mercury ☿—corresponding to body, soul, and spirit. Others would have it be the four elements, saying that the "conversion" of these elements is the entirety of the work. And some again would have it be a "fifth essence" <note>the quintessence</note> and a quintessential spiritual body. They say their mastery and mystery consist in these five numbers: 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5. These appear in my mentioned epitaph and circular shields, understood through the initial "chaos" and the resulting products.

<summary>The author explains how alchemical symbols and the Elixir relate to both physical health and spiritual redemption, outlining various philosophical theories on whether the "Stone" is composed of one, two, three, four, or five essential parts.</summary>

...indeed, it may be only one specific thing and one continuous, simple, and single operation, once it is duly prepared and excesses are removed. But if only one thing is taken, it is then divided into several parts; or, if several things are taken, they are brought together as one. The same may be said of the Operation: although it is entirely one, the Philosophers nevertheless choose to distinguish it by its various stages, colors, and properties. These are suggested by and within the three illustrated circles and their titles. All of this agrees with the old riddle <note>original: "Ænygma"</note> of Vitriol. While vitriol <note>a sulfate of a metal; in alchemy, it often refers to the "Green Lion" or the beginning material of the Work</note> exists in many metallic forms, it consists of only one substance. Although it is but one thing, it may be opened, divided, and separated into several parts; once this is done, it can be brought back to one again through a single and simple operation of Nature. Thus:

->*Translated into English as follows:*<-

Take this *Arcadian* <note>referring to Mercury or Hermes, the messenger god associated with the volatile metal mercury</note> slippery lad, who is prone to fly away,
And let him die, drowned in the glittering *Stygian* lake <note>the "Stygian Lake" refers to a transformative alchemical bath or solvent, named after the river Styx in the underworld</note>;
When the juices of *Hyas* <note>original: "Hials"; likely a reference to moisture or the "philosophical water"</note> are in his breast, God saves him from loss.
Lift him up and fix him to a cross where *Lemnian* earth <note>a medicinal clay from the island of Lemnos, here representing a stable mineral base</note> nourishes him;
Then, buried in a warm cave, dissolve what is rotten,
From whose sinews the drops of this, our "Body," are obtained.
Spirits will pierce through and bring this offspring out of the shadows in an orderly way,
Clothed all around with gold and silver.
At length, cast this upon live coals, and you will soon see
Another renewed (like a *Phoenix*) by that process;
This, with its touch alone, perfects all bodies here,
Surpassing the strict bonds and laws of Nature's sphere;
It will change the species to a higher degree,
Whereby all grief may cease, and poverty shall flee.

And yet, understand me correctly concerning the mentioned work and the "matter of the Philosophers" <note>the raw material used to create the Philosopher's Stone</note>. Gold is certainly the principle of the gold-making powder (in whatever subject or appearance it may take), just as fire is the principle of burning. For nothing can give what it does not possess. *In gold are the seeds of gold* <note>original: "In Auro, semina sunt Auri."</note>, as Augurellus <note>Giovanni Aurelio Augurello (c. 1441–1524), an Italian poet and alchemist</note> and others testify. And even the same may be said of *Lune* <note>Silver (symbolized by the Moon)</note> when it is in a masculine form. Their *Mercury* is the foundation of both and contains all three; it is the "Earth" in which it is sown and from which it takes its origin, and it is of their own nature. But this must be "living" gold or silver, and not common gold and silver, which are "dead," nor common, foul quicksilver <note>mercury</note>. Indeed, these [living versions] are more universal...

<meta>This page continues the author's argument that the "First Matter" of the Philosopher’s Stone is not a rare or expensive substance, but something common and often overlooked, found throughout nature.</meta>

[The matter is] cheaper, more common, and easier to obtain than most men—even some philosophers—think. This led the ingenious and learned Taulodanus <note>Robertus Taulodanus, an alchemical author</note> to write against the subject chosen by that worthy old philosopher Bracescus <note>Giovanni Bracesco, an Italian alchemist</note>, though both were true philosophers and their respective subjects were valid. This also led Claveus <note>Gaston de Clave, a French chemist and alchemist</note>, in his works *Chrysopeia* <note>original: "Chrysopeia"; the art of making gold</note> and *Argyropeia* <note>original: "Argyropeia"; the art of making silver</note>, to doubt some of Ramon Lully's processes.

For these principles are found in one specific substance, as well as in various others that possess a "golden nature," as Dunstan, Arnold, Guido, Ripley, Raymond, Glauber, and others testify. There is more than one way to reach the goal <note>original: "more ways are to the Wood then one"</note>. Through proper philosophical preparation, the raw material for the Philosopher’s Stone can be extracted from any specific metallic or mineral species. Any chemical work categorized as a <term>particular</term> <note>a "particular" work in alchemy aimed at a specific transformation or a "lesser" transmutation, rather than the "universal" medicine</note> can—through proper preparation, purification, <term>fusible fixation</term> <note>making a substance stable under heat so that it melts without evaporating</note>, <term>volatilization</term> <note>turning a solid into vapor</note>, and <term>exaltation</term> <note>the process of raising a substance to a higher state of purity or power</note>—be transformed into a "universal work" for <term>multiplication</term> <note>the final stage of the Great Work where the power or quantity of the Stone is increased</note.

Indeed, from every element and principle within nature—and from almost any lowly or discarded thing—one can extract a "sulfurous" Sun (*Sol*), Moon (*Luna*), or Mercury <note>referring to the three alchemical principles of Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt, or the metaphorical seeds of gold and silver</note> that is enlivened for the philosopher’s Great Work. Sendivogius <note>original: "Se. Devogius"; referring to Michael Sendivogius, a famous Polish alchemist</note> affirms that this "First Matter" of the philosophers is easier to touch with the hand than it is to perceive or find through subtle wit or over-complicated imaginations. He says he explained both the substance and the process literally to some who, nevertheless, had no confidence in it because it seemed so common and lowly; therefore, they abandoned it without even trying.

Certainly, the preceding and primordial "Essence of Gold" <note>original: "Ens Auri"</note> exists in every element and principle. These elements are never so simple that one cannot be extracted from another. We can see a demonstration of this in our "Mother Earth," who produces all things. Take any good, suitable soil and remove all the stones, roots, <term>saltpeter</term> <note>potassium nitrate, a mineral salt often associated with the "vital air" or "universal spirit" in alchemy</note>, and everything else it contains. If you then leave it open to the air for some time in a suitable place, it will not only become impregnated again with new

<summary>The author argues that the "First Matter" of alchemy is a common, inexpensive substance found in all elements and mineral species, though many seekers overlook it because of its perceived low value. He cites various authorities and uses the example of soil regenerating its minerals from the air to prove that the essence of gold is omnipresent in nature.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author’s argument that the fundamental principles of the Philosopher’s Stone can be found in common substances across all three kingdoms of nature—animal, vegetable, and mineral—provided they are correctly processed.</meta>

Saltpeter, vegetables, stones, metals, and minerals—but also animals—contain a sulfurous gold and mercury. These are suitable for a philosopher to work with to create a proper medicine for any of the three kingdoms of nature. Once this substance is specifically combined with a suitable metal, it shall perfect impure metals into Sol <note>original: "Sol"; gold</note> and Lune <note>original: "Lune"; silver</note>. It is remarkable that saltpeter, though found as a mineral in the earth, has its origins and source in the air.

Truly, anything brought to such a state of elemental perfection and balanced in the "three principles"—becoming both <term>quintessential</term> <note>the "fifth essence," the purest and most concentrated form of a substance</note> and <term>fixed</term> <note>stable under heat; non-volatile</note>—shares the same underlying substance as metals. These substances are universal in nature and can help create the "Stone" for the transmutation of metals as well as for human health. 

All things share a common matter consisting of Sal <note>original: "Sal"; salt</note>, Sulfur, and Mercury. The purity of the four elements is found in pure water and pure earth when they are refined into a quintessential essence. Thus, they share the same substance as metals and possess the same fundamental nature as their principles: namely, salt, sulfur, and mercury. 

Since the materials and principles of <term>generation</term> <note>the process of creation or growth in nature</note> are found in salt, sulfur, and mercury, these can be obtained through "Art" <note>alchemy</note> above the ground just as easily as they are formed by nature within the mines. They can then be transformed into a "fixed sulfur of nature," which serves as an excellent "earth" for the alchemical work. 

As Guido <note>Guido de Montanor, a 14th-century alchemist</note> says of this "earth," the specific type does not matter as long as it is fixed. Raymond <note>Raymond Lully, a major figure in alchemical literature</note> says that nothing is required for the art of transmutation except pure earth and pure water. Ripley <note>George Ripley, a famous 15th-century English alchemist</note> notes that while hair and blood themselves cannot be the "Stone" for transmutation, the elements extracted from them can be. The mercury <note>original: "☿"</note> extracted from them is of little use on its own, but if it is transformed into the "sulfur of nature," it becomes an ideal "earth" for the process. However, take note: it must be brought into a common nature with metals and must be fermented with actual, pure gold. Yet, you are not forced to start with substances so distantly related; things that are more "near of kin" to metals are easier to work with.

Drink three, or thrice three, which is a mystery.

This grows up to the highest degree of the power, energy, and virtue of silver <note>original symbol: ☽</note> and gold, and of the spiritual Stone of Philosophers, to do whatever else the philosophers require. 

"For the Philosophers' Stone is nothing other than gold multiplied in its degrees, while maintaining the same proportion it had in the original gold" <note>original Latin: *Nam Lapis Philosophorum nihil aliud est quam Aurum in gradibus suis multiplicatum stante proportione quâ fuit in Auro primo*</note>. For the philosophers' treasure is no other thing than gold multiplied in its degrees, remaining in the same temperature or proportion in which it was at the first. This must be nourished with the mother's pure milk until it can feed upon stronger foods <note>original: "Meats"</note>, and so gains the strength to multiply. 

And then the glorified King—triply crowned—shall vanquish his enemies and redeem his brothers and kindred in any and all nations from their vile corruptions, if they can but touch the hem of his garment or welcome him as they should upon his arrival. For it is all the same to him whether he raises up most essences or removes their illnesses. Yet you...

First, learn the "eagles" <note>original: "Eagles"; volatile substances, often sal ammoniac, used to sublimate or lift fixed matter</note> that nourish the "doves" <note>original: "Doves"; the white, purified stage of the alchemical matter</note>,

Without the perfect knowledge of which, you can never attain your end.

# CHAPTER 2

## Concerning the Causes and Manner of the Multiplication of Life and Seed; and one way of preparing Mercury for the Philosopher's Stone, and others for making Universal Medicines, etc.

In the beginning, God gave His blessing to increase and multiply, and commanded that each thing should draw its form from its own kind. Thus, He created in Nature a certain chain, or subordinate closeness of temperaments <note>original: "propinquity of Complexions"</note>, between visible and invisible things. By this chain, the superior spiritual essences descend and interact here below with physical matter.

Yet Nature has not, and never had, more than one single agent (hidden in the universe), which is the *Soul of the World* <note>original Latin: *Anima Mundi*</note>. This soul works through its universal Spirit through innumerable distinct "concreates" <note>things created together or at the same time</note> according to their specific forms and seeds, which God the Father established at the first Creation by His Word and Idea (or Son and Holy Spirit)...

<summary>This page uses alchemical allegories—involving figures like Diana, Mars, and Cupid—to describe the chemical processes required for the "Great Work." It explains that all natural multiplication follows a divine law where spiritual essences interact with matter via the "Soul of the World."</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, Eagles and Doves, Diana, Venus, Mars, Anima Mundi, Soul of the World, multiplication, spiritual essences, Chalybs, Gordian Knot</keywords>

--- Page 41 ---
<meta>This page continues the philosophical discussion of how God’s divine providence governs the natural world through the concept of "seeds" or essential life-forces within all matter.</meta>

[He] glanced at once into the first matter, and so established the laws and boundaries of nature. He still presides over all of this—in every part and over the whole—upholding, strengthening, and ordering all these powers as His instruments in every specific detail as well as in general. So, not even a sparrow falls without His providence and power. In this way, like produces like in all of nature’s *Three Kingdoms* (*Animal*, *Vegetable*, and *Mineral*) by means of this "seed." 

For as *Fernelius* <note>original: Jean Fernel (1497–1558), a French physician who sought to bridge the gap between philosophy and medicine</note> says, "There is nothing in any part of nature that does not contain within itself the seed of its own kind" <note>original: *Nihil est in ulla naturæ parte, quod non in se generis sui semen contineat*</note>. God and Nature still use this seed as a means to unite "Form" <note>the spiritual or organizing principle of a thing</note> to its own "Matter," and to awaken strength and "appetite" <note>the natural drive or tendency of a substance toward change</note> in the "patient" <note>the passive material being acted upon</note>, inviting the active power of Form and Life to work freely. 

Yet its movements still tend toward the specific goal God ordained, unless it is misplaced or abused (as *Sendivogius* <note>original: Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist and chemist</note> explains) or joined to some unsuitable matter. Once that goal is reached, the life-force then appears dead or at a standstill. It is so chained, hedged in, and imprisoned by physical barriers <note>original: "Corporal Fences"</note> that it can no longer work to improve that particular subject. Instead, it only "organizes the mass" <note>original: *Organizare molem*</note> and sets its "prison" or "house" (the physical body) in the best order it can. It branches out into various members so that it may have more room to use its faculties, as is clearly seen in the various movements of *Animals* and *Vegetables*. 

But in *Minerals*—which are more weighed down by matter—this is less apparent. They seem "slain" by being frozen solid <note>original: "congelation"</note>, especially when they are taken out of their mines and used for mechanical purposes. In that state, the mineral only preserves its physical existence until it is revived with a new "ferment" <note>a substance used to trigger a chemical or alchemical transformation</note> and matter. Through this, the body is opened again to reveal its living, essential moisture hidden at the center—where the seed and spirit of life are placed like a fire—and then it revives and restores new operations within the newly added nourishment or matter. And thus Nature, with the help of Art <note>the skill and practice of alchemy</note>, may transcend...

<summary>This page describes how divine providence operates through "seeds" in the animal, vegetable, and mineral kingdoms, explaining that alchemical "Art" can revive the life-force trapped within "frozen" minerals.</summary>

->*For Form is Light, the source of central heat,*<-
->*Which, clothed with matter, does a seed beget;*<-
->*Wherein Life, like fire, seeks to increase itself,*<-
->*And make itself eternal, if the fuel never ceases.*<-

Helmont <note>original: Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644), a Flemish chemist and physician</note> in his writing about *Butler* <note>a reference to a famous account of an Irishman who possessed a miraculous healing stone</note>, and Sendivogius <note>original: Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a Polish alchemist</note> in his book *The New Light of Alchemy*, partly testify to this same truth. As soon as this seed is produced, it attempts to change the matter and stamps its character within it. Immediately, the matter comes to life and then works together with the Form to attain the goal for which the implanted seed is intended.

->*For all things live according to their kind,*<-
->*Their Life is Light, as you may find within.*<-

->*As much Light as anything has, so much of the Divine it possesses,*<-

(as one author says). This concludes the discussion of Form and Seed in general.

Understand further that metals in the mineral kingdom are produced in this way: Their oily <note>original: "unctuous"</note> sulfur coagulates and fixes a flowing mineral moisture called *mercury*. This mercury is a "dry humidity" that flows but does not wet the hands. Its parts are so uniform <note>original: "homogeneal"</note> that even fire itself does not easily separate them. It is descended from water, yet far exceeds it in weight and firm composition. These properties do not happen by chance, but by God’s decree, providence, and power—stemming from its specific seed and its hidden inward agent, Form, and Life, derived from the *World Soul* <note>original: *Anima Mundi*, the divine essence that alchemists believed permeated all nature</note>, which the water did not previously possess. Nor does it have distinct parts (like a hand, foot, head, or eye) as animals do, or other structures like vegetables; rather, it is entirely uniform and consists of very stable parts and roots. Now, Mercury...

The author argues that all things possess a degree of divinity proportional to the "light" or life-force they contain.</summary>

--- Page 43 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of how mercury interacts with other metals and the alchemical process of "reincrudation"—returning a metal to its raw, "living" state to release its inner power.</meta>

Mercury has the most affinity with gold, as shown by their equal weight, purity, firm structure, and easy mixture. Next is its affinity with silver, then Jupiter <note>original: "Jove"—the alchemical name for tin</note>, Saturn <note>lead</note>, Venus <note>copper</note>, and last and least with Mars <note>iron</note>, which is a secret to understand. 

And though mercury may be mixed and made into an amalgam with any or all of these, it will not enter into the "root" of any of them without proper preparation and great art. Instead, the fire will drive one away from the other, which is another secret. Now, the reason for this is that both the mercury and the metals are "dead," or their life is hidden, imprisoned, and dormant within their bodies (as has been said). The sulfur is fixed and sealed in the perfect metals, but in the imperfect metals, it is earthly and foul <note>original: "Fowl"—likely an error for "foul" or "feculent," meaning impure or drossy</note> or raw. Mercury abhors and rejects this, or cannot cope with it, being itself also in fetters and bound to good behavior. 

Even if you separate the dross <note>original: "faces"—likely from the Latin *faeces*, meaning dregs or impurities</note> from these imperfect metals, you still only have a fluid mercury like the common sort and a raw sulfur too distant in nature to join with gold. For gold, having moved past its "enchantments" <note>the initial raw or "crude" state of a metal</note> and impurities, scorns to be defiled by them any longer. Therefore, common raw sulfur will more easily join with other imperfect metals than with gold, but pure and fixed sulfur joins sooner and better with gold than with the rest.

Therefore, if you wish to use the sulfurs or mercuries of the imperfect metals or common materials, each must be prepared and fitted with a living power. They must be so sharpened <note>original: "acuate"—to make chemically active or potent</note> that they become a fiery, life-giving agent before they can return to a raw state <note>original: "reincrudate"—to take a "perfect" metal and turn it back into its "crude" or liquid beginnings</note>, open, and enter the body of the Sun <note>original: "Sol"—gold</note>. Only then can gold's own "water" appear, and its fiery seed and spirit of life come forth to be made active. It can then work upon and within that "female" living mercury, which is gold’s own essence, flesh, and bone. This is its proper substance, earth, and womb <note>original: "Matrix"</note>, in which the seed will then quickly bear fruit and increase. For gold (though pure, perfect, and full of virtue in its physical body) must be returned to a raw state, crucified, and die to nature, so that its locked-up virtue and transformative essence <note>original: "Tincture"</note>...

<summary>This page explains the chemical and symbolic relationships between mercury and other metals, emphasizing that gold must be "broken down" or "crucified" to release its reproductive alchemical seed.</summary>
<keywords>mercury, gold, sulfur, reincrudation, Sol, alchemy, affinity, planetary metals, tincture</keywords>

...and only single in itself bodily, might become exalted with its body and, spiritually living, and fixed together in heavenly mansions. In this way, it may extend and communicate more largely its powerful virtues and <term>tincture</term> <note>original: "Tincture"—in alchemy, a potent substance or "essence" capable of infused transformation, whether turning base metals into gold or healing the human body</note> to imperfect bodies and spirits to redeem them from bondage, corruption, and fire by embracing one grain of his bounteous pure spirit, and so be raised at last to him for eternity. For in this process, the death and destruction of the outward form will be but as a back door to the soul and spirit’s true birth, and its body's eternal life and union, until it comes at last to the highest perfection by its fullness of tincture. 

Thus is the <term>Philosophical Corner-stone</term> <note>A reference to the Philosopher’s Stone, here equated with the biblical "chief cornerstone," symbolizing the ultimate achievement in both alchemy and spiritual piety</note> made a true medicine, though rejected and scoffed at by many. And these are the effects hidden from the voluptuous, covetous, and worldly-wise philosophers, and revealed to solitary, meek, and humble spirits who forsake outward pomp and vanities to embrace the fruits of piety and wisdom.

Now observe further that everything that is convertible into gold has its *Mercury* and *Sulphur*, which either is or may be <note>original: "acuate"—meaning sharpened, activated, or made chemically reactive</note> activated and made fiery and living for a philosophical preparation of and with *Sol* <note>original: "Sol"—Latin for "Sun," the alchemical symbol for gold</note>. In this way, both the common and metallic mercury may be thus fitted and prepared to wed with *Sol*. All of these mercuries (as has been said) are beforehand in themselves "dead." For the preparation of mercury is done in this way: namely, by a mineral with black <note>original: "sable"</note> silver veins, which is the Dragon born in <term>Saturn’s Den</term> <note>In alchemical allegory, the "Dragon" often refers to antimony or crude ores, and "Saturn" refers to lead; the "Den" implies the raw, earthbound state of the mineral</note>, devouring Cadmus with his earthly men.

> Must yet be pierced by him, he being the God of Wars.
> Then both will perish and become a Star,
> Where the young King is born, who is Solar <note>associated with the sun/gold</note>.
> Then wash equal Venus <note>copper</note> in his blood, and let
> Them join, until Vulcan <note>the god of fire, representing the heat of the furnace</note> takes them in a net.

### CHAPTER III.

## Concerning the Subject and Marks of the Immortal Liquor, Alkahest.

Reader, pause here for a moment and take this short hint for your true instruction regarding the **Alkahest** <note>original: "Alchahest"—a hypothetical universal solvent sought by alchemists, popularized by Jan Baptista van Helmont</note> and **Maccabean Fire** <note>A reference to a story in 2 Maccabees (in the Apocrypha), where a sacred fire was hidden in a dry pit and later recovered as a thick, oily liquid</note>, which burns in water and lies hidden like a serpent (or **fluid** <note>original: "Latex"—Latin for a liquid or hidden spring; here it refers to a secret alchemical solvent</note>) in the caverns of the Earth, as well as in other things and places. It is, nevertheless, only one anomalous, balsamic salt passing through the world, which almost every person knows and needs, though they do not notice the signs that identify it. 

I say it is the **first essence of salts** <note>original: "Primum ens Salium"—the primary being or fundamental essence of all salts</note> and has a mark or cross placed upon it by the Almighty. As Helmont <note>Jan Baptista van Helmont (1580–1644), a pioneering chemist and physician who coined the term "gas" and wrote extensively on the Alkahest</note> says, the **Adepts** <note>original: "Adept"—one who has mastered the secrets of alchemy</note> know this mark, and every diligent philosophical searcher may find it to be a sure and certain token of its true alkahestical virtue, beyond any demonstration. Indeed, we must not seek or expect to find something in a substance that God and Nature have not implanted within it. For nothing can give

<summary>This page concludes a poetic instruction on the union of Mercury and Sol (gold) and introduces the Alkahest, described as a "balsamic salt" and the "first essence of salts" marked by divine sign.</summary>
<keywords>Mercury, Sol, Alkahest, Maccabean Fire, Jan Baptista van Helmont, Primum ens Salium, alchemical solvent, balsamic salt</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "mark" or sign that identifies the primary substance of the Alkahest. (17)

[it cannot] give what it does not have. But its virtue, operation, and power may be clarified and exalted by art <note>The "art" refers to the practice of alchemy or chemistry</note>. I say, then, that this mark is not the mark of Cain <note>In Genesis, a sign of punishment; here, the author contrasts it with a life-giving sign</note> or any beastly curse, but quite the opposite; it can preserve life so that nothing can kill it, even if something tried to devour it. Until you find this mark, you "shoot at rovers" <note>original: "shoot at Rovers"—an archery term for shooting at random or distant targets; here, it means guessing blindly</note>. Although the donkey bears such an outward mark with ignominy <note>A reference to the cross-shaped marking on a donkey's back, often interpreted as a sign of its service to Christ</note>, Christ was pleased to ride upon it and later to grace the Cross through the mighty power of his suffering, possessing a constant, balsamic <note>healing or soothing</note> virtue of patience while upon it.

Some light is shed on this mark and token by both *Paracelsus* <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a seminal Swiss physician and alchemist</note>, the glory of *chemists*, and by the brave *Helmont*, his great interpreter. However, they hid this information away from common observation <note>original: "Rustick observation"</note> in appropriate places. Yet their preparations are plainly described as being only simple dissolution and coagulation <note>The process of liquefying a solid and then solidifying it again</note> with gentle heat, until it reaches its transmuted form without any mixable ferment that is foreign <note>original: "Heterogeneous"</note> to itself.

But this **Serpent biting his own tail** <note>The Ouroboros, an ancient symbol of infinity and the cyclic nature of the alchemical work</note> becomes venomous through digestion and putrefaction <note>The stage of decomposition in the alchemical process</note>; thus, by solution, it is "mortified" <note>In alchemy, to "mortify" means to destroy the external form of a substance to release its internal essence</note> into the smallest atoms possible in nature. It is then raised, circulated, and revived for eternity into some higher state <note>original: "Orb"</note> or Elixir. In this state, it cannot mix with any basic impurity or be changed by another ferment. Instead, it separates and preserves every essential substance <note>original: "concrete"</note> it is joined with from corruption and the causes of death, without any loss of its own or their original created virtue.

## CHAPTER IV.

# *Of the Salt of Tartar volatilized, or Samech, and other Elixirs.*

I may tell you here, however, that although the proper substance of this previous liquid, called the *Alkahest*, is only one irregular <note>original: "Anomalous"</note> salt—the first origin of salts—which bears such a noted mark and, like John the Baptist, performs such great or mighty works, yet nevertheless the least perfected elixir <note>original: "Elixirated subject"</note> in the Alchemists' <note>original: "Philosophers"</note> Kingdom (though it is the lowest refined salt) will produce such Alkahest-like effects, and some even greater. This is especially true when it is caught up (like the Apostle Paul) from the four <note>original: "Quaternary"</note> elements into the crystalline "third heavens" above the fixed stars and planetary spheres. 

For the highly prepared Samech <note>A term used by Paracelsus for the salt of tartar (potassium carbonate) when it has been refined or "volatized"</note> of *Paracelsus*, and every alkaline salt made from the ashes <note>original: "Alcalifate Incinerated"</note> of plant-based spirits <note>original: "wine of vegetables"</note>, when brought to their full preparation and perfection, are like the Alkahest—or at least serve as a substitute <note>original: "Succedaneous"</note>, like a "lesser circulation" <note>original: "Circulatum minus"—a specific alchemical solvent for vegetable substances</note>. This also applies to all other healing <note>original: "Balſamick"</note>, concentrated <note>original: "Quinteſſential"</note> substances and solid forms <note>original: "Concretes"</note> in the three universal kingdoms of nature <note>The animal, vegetable, and mineral kingdoms</note>. 

But more specifically, the true mercurial, saline, and sulfurous elixirs of the alchemists—when worked up and raised to the bright crystalline or angelic spheres—act as spiritual, meltable liquid forms and appearances. These are so universally like the Alkahest that I say they may do the same things, if not greater, and produce better, more refined healing <note>original: "Balſamick"</note> separations and preparations than the ordinary salt-based Alkahest. But the method of preparation (and the manner of arrangement <note>original: "modus diſpoſitionis"</note>) must be considered to bring this to pass. For the ranks of the hierarchy contribute greatly to the glory of angelic powers and influences. And yet the aforementioned Alkahest (as a good forerunner)

forerunner) may prepare the way or foundation for this grand **Elixir**. There, they will later live in peace upon heavenly **manna** in **Paradise**, and tend the **Garden of Eden** with new plants, delighting in all the fruits of life. They shall have an **angelic guardian** and **gardener** with a **flaming sword** to prevent and keep out all crude <note>original: "Rustick"</note> and malevolent followers and pursuers.

And Reader, this greater secret may be revealed here: some affirm that all the concrete substances <note>original: "Concreats"</note> and things in the three kingdoms of nature (**animal**, **vegetable**, and **mineral**) may be reduced to such a quintessential perfection of the four **elements** and three **principles** <note>The three principles are Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</note> that they achieve a community of nature. In this state, they will provide the material for the **Philosophers' Stone** in any form. However, they must then be **fermented** with **gold** and **silver** to be used for **metals** and **minerals**; in this way, they may easily transmute coarse metals into **gold** or **silver**, and perfect baser **minerals** and **stones**, just as they may exalt their own **specific** kinds.

## CHAP. V

### *An Apologetic Conclusion on Human Mortality, Resurrection, and our Eternal State.* <note>original: "Apologitical Peroration"</note>

Perhaps some may say that it is not easy to find or understand everything written in this short volume through individual experiments or published books. I confess this is true; I could not understand it myself until I had the blessing of speaking with certain philosophical authors and heard their spoken explanations <note>original: "living words"</note> to demonstrate these truths. Through them, I also understood and discovered the hidden meanings <note>original: "concealments"</note> of **Paracelsus** <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a pioneer of the medical use of chemicals</note> and **Van Helmont** <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644), a Flemish chemist and physician</note>, for which I have provided you a key here. If this helps you, acknowledge it as a gift from God. If you dislike it, your time and expense will not be much harmed by these few lines—they could have been spent on worse things. Take it as a gesture of good intentions, or however else you please, as long as you do not lose your Christian character through envy or by speaking ill of things you do not understand.

<summary>The author concludes his technical alchemical descriptions by stating that the Philosopher's Stone encompasses all other preparations, then begins a concluding chapter defending his work and crediting his knowledge to divine blessing and the study of Paracelsus and Van Helmont.</summary>
<keywords>Philosopher's Stone, Butler's Stone, solvent, Paracelsus, Van Helmont, alchemy, mortality, chemical medicine</keywords>

<sig>C 3</sig>

And so, I hope that in this short discourse I have sufficiently explained my enigmatic <note>original: "ænygmatical"</note> philosophical shields <note>original: "Scutcheons"; heraldic emblems used here as symbols for alchemical secrets</note> and my epitaph, along with the universal solvent <note>original: "Alchahest"</note>, the spirit of salt <note>original: "Samech"; an alchemical term likely referring to a fixed salt or tartar used as a solvent</note>, and other elixirs, as well as the accompanying words and diagrams. If you are more curious, I leave the rest to be explained by the authors I mentioned previously and the many others who are more experienced in this art. 

If you still blame me because you do not understand these writings, or my diagrams and epitaph, I am resolved to remain as silent as a dead man. For if I deserve blame, I should bear it quietly; if not, I am used to scandals and insults—ranging from the heights of a king's court <note>original: "Pharaohs Court"</note> to the depths of a trash heap <note>original: "Jobs Dunghil"; a biblical reference to the extreme suffering and humiliation of the prophet Job</note>—and I can consider it a glory to suffer patiently. I have written down what the philosophers and masters <note>original: "Adeptists"; those who have successfully achieved the "Great Work" of alchemy</note> have stated and admitted, both in person <note>original: "viva voce"</note> and in print. Neither they nor I could convey this knowledge in the plainest words without the specific inspiration of God. 

Therefore, if you desire this great blessing, ask it of Him who gives generously and does not find fault when it serves His glory. But be sure to prepare yourself through purity and holiness, with true self-denial <note>original: "mortification"</note>, if you wish for your work to succeed and thrive. Therefore, pray with sincerity that God—in and through the spirit of Christ—may bring you to life from useless deeds <note>original: "dead works"</note> and separate light from your dark body and the chaos of sin. In this way, being truly baptized into Him and His righteousness by a vital and living seed of faith, you may improve your talent and rise through and above this corrupting material world <note>original: "quaternary defiling world"; referring to the four physical elements</note> into the power of the Trinity <note>original: "Trivne power"</note>. Finally, you may reach the quintessential <note>The "fifth element" or purest state of being beyond the material world</note> or super-celestial central circle of peace and heavenly bliss <note>original: "Beatitude"</note>.

Now, kind reader, if you are not satisfied with this work or these expressions, leave them to the author. For the aforementioned epitaph and illustrated shields will serve well enough as my gravestone (which was my primary intention for them at the start), where I may lie at rest,

...with or without any other heraldry or applause. In this, you may nevertheless plainly read of your mortality, just as you would on other tombs, to prepare yourself for your long home of eternity. For your body, soul, and spirit must be separated, and the four elements—having been corrupted from the salt <note>original: "Sal"</note>, sulfur <note>original: "Sulphur"</note>, and mercury <note>original: "Mercury"</note>—generate worms, and so on. These, after a full and perfect separation, are to be reunited again at the Day of Judgment <note>original: "day of doom"</note> for a quintessential, super-celestial, and everlasting existence.

The good will exist in the joy and peace of the Holy Spirit <note>original: "Holy Ghost"</note>, which, through righteousness in this life, had transformed <note>original: "fermented"; in alchemy, fermentation refers to a process of spiritual or physical refinement</note> them into members of Christ’s body. This spirit was in all the saints and the true universal church <note>original: "Catholick Church"</note>—the hope of glory. 

But the others, who were bad, are left to their bestial, sensual, and devilish corrupted <note>original: "fermented"</note> affections. They are to be tormented eternally by their evil spirits and grosser essences for their idolatry of fleshly, devilish, and worldly vanities. They will face horror and everlasting anguish of mind and body; nevertheless, they will be sustained by these very things so they are able to endure forever and ever. 

I have declared all of this, and it cannot be easily hidden from you, even if you lacked the sharp vision of Lynceus <note>Lynceus was an Argonaut in Greek mythology famous for his superhuman eyesight</note> or the eye of the philosopher's eagle <note>a symbol of spiritual and alchemical insight</note> to behold the light of nature exalted to the highest degree of the Sun <note>the "Sun" or gold represents the ultimate perfection in the alchemical process</note> through art. Nevertheless, I wish that you might find this out by these or other means, so that it may tend to the praise and honor of God and the eternal welfare of you and your neighbor. I am your friend and a true lover of art and nature, and I do not care what you say or think of ***W. C.*** <note>likely the initials of William Cooper, a prominent 17th-century stationer and alchemical publisher</note> or "twice five hundred" <note>a cryptic play on his initials; in Roman numerals, "C" equals 100, and "twice five hundred" equals 1,000, or "M"</note>.

# To the Reader.

...dolph *Glauber* <note>Johann Rudolph Glauber (1604–1670), a German-Dutch alchemist and pioneer of chemical technology</note>, *Basilius Valentinus* <note>A legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings were influential in the 17th century</note>, and *Michael Sendivogius* <note>original: "Cosmopolite Sendivogius"; a Polish alchemist and diplomat famous for his work on air and salts</note>, the brave *van Helmont* <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644), a Flemish physician and founder of pneumatic chemistry</note>, and *Paracelsus*, along with several other translations into **English**. In these works, many rare secrets are revealed for the honor of this art, the improvement of our **English** nation, and to establish a belief in the aforementioned Stone <note>The Philosopher's Stone</note>.

This worthy *Helvetius* <note>Johann Friedrich Schweitzer (1630–1709), a Dutch physician whose account of a successful transmutation became famous</note>, it seems, formerly had a misconception <note>original: "misprision"; meaning he held the art in contempt or misunderstood its value</note> of this art. However, by the demonstrations mentioned in his book, he was convinced. He worthily recanted his former views to prefer the Truth and God’s honor over his own reputation, through which he has gained even more respect among all virtuous and learned men. 

Now, if these experiments gain the same credit with you (as I have no doubt they will), you will not in any way disparage or scandalize this almost **divine art**. Nevertheless, I do not persuade you (like the murmuring, **idolatrous Jews**) to adore this **Ass** or **Golden Calf** (the workmanship of men's hands), even though it is called...

# THE EPISTLE DEDICATORY
## By Doctor *John Frederick Helvetius*.
### To the most Excellent and Learned Doctors: Dr. *Theodosius Retius* in *Amsterdam*, Doctor *John Casper Fausius* in *Heidelberg*, and Doctor *Christianus Mentzelius* in *Brandenburg*, My Honored Friends and Patrons.

Most noble and insightful seekers of <term>Vulcanick Anatomy</term> <note>original: "Vulcanick Anatomy"; a reference to the study of chemistry or alchemy through the use of fire, named after Vulcan, the Roman god of fire and the forge</note>: I do not wish to be lacking in demonstrating the glory and riches of this ancient <term>Spagyrick Art</term> <note>original: "Spagyrick Art"; an early term for alchemy, specifically the process of separating and then recombining the components of substances to create medicines or transmutations</note>. This is an art which I have personally witnessed and performed by applying a very small amount of the <term>Transmuting Powder</term> <note>often called the "Philosopher's Stone," this was the legendary substance believed to change base metals into gold</note> to a piece of impure lead. In a moment, that lead was transformed into the most stable and pure gold, withstanding the most rigorous testing by fire. Because of this, no one should doubt, but rather know for certain, that the primary material, the <term>Mercury of Philosophers</term> <note>not common liquid mercury, but a theoretical "first matter" that alchemists believed was the essential starting point for creating the Philosopher's Stone</note>, can indeed be found; it is like a fountain overflowing with wonderful effects. However, it is not my intention to teach anyone this art—of which I myself am still ignorant—but only to recount the processes I have witnessed. For it is only the part

# CHAPTER I.

Before I describe the "Philosophical Pygmy" <note>This likely refers to the Philosopher's Stone, which was often described as a small, unassuming substance capable of performing "giant" feats of transformation.</note> conquering giants in this Theater of Secrets, allow me to transcribe some of *Helmont's* <note>original: "Helmonts"; Jan Baptista van Helmont (1580–1644) was a pioneering Flemish chemist and physician.</note> words from his book, *The Tree of Life*, page 630.

I am compelled (he says) to believe there is a Stone to make gold and silver, even though I know many excellent chemists <note>original: "Chymiſts"</note> have consumed their own and other men’s wealth in search of this mystery. To this day (alas), we see these unwary and simple laborers <term>Laborants</term> <note>individuals who perform the manual work in a laboratory</note> cunningly deluded by a diabolical crew of gold- and silver-sucking flies or leeches. 

But I know many foolish <note>original: "Stupid"</note> men will contradict this truth. One man will claim it is a work of the devil; another, a hodgepodge; another, the "soul of gold," believing that one ounce of this gold may transform <note>original: "tinged"</note> only one ounce of lead and no more. But this is contrary <note>original: "repugnant"</note> to the testimony of *Kiffler* <note>likely referring to a contemporary chemist or associate of the Alchemist Glauber</note> and others, as I shall show you. Another perhaps believes it is possible but says, "The sauce is more expensive than the meat" <note>an idiom meaning the cost of production exceeds the value of the result</note>. Yet I do not wonder at this at all, for according to the proverb,

->*Things that we do not understand, we admire;*<-
->*But things that please our fancy, we desire.*<-

Now what will man do in the study of natural things—man, who has fallen from the fountain of light into the bottomless pit of darkness? This is especially true in this philosophical study of nature. Indeed, if people understand a thing, they despise it, not knowing that there is more to be sought than what is already possessed. For this reason, *Seneca* <note>Lucius Annaeus Seneca (4 BC – 65 AD), a Roman Stoic philosopher.</note> was right when he said in his *Book of Manners* <note>original: "book of Manners"; likely referring to the *Liber de Moribus*, a collection of proverbs attributed to Seneca in the early modern period.</note>, "You are not yet happy if the unrefined <note>original: "ruder sort"</note> do not mock <note>original: "deride"</note> you." 

But whether men believe, mock, or contradict, the transmutation of metals is a certainty. For my own eyes have seen it, and my hands have done it; I have handled this spark of God's everlasting wisdom, or the true universal <term>Catholick</term> <note>In this context, 'catholic' means universal or all-encompassing, rather than referring to the Roman Catholic Church.</note> "Saturnine Magnesia" <term>Magnesia of Philosophers</term> <note>A term in alchemy for the primary substance or "First Matter" used to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note>—a literal fire powerful enough to pierce rocks, and a treasure equivalent to twenty tons of gold. What more do you seek? I believed it with the "eyes of Thomas" in my fingers <note>A reference to the Apostle Thomas, who needed to touch Jesus' wounds to believe; the author is saying he has physical, tactile proof.</note>. I have seen, I say, in nature that most secret, supernatural, magical "Saturn" <note>In alchemy, Saturn often refers to lead or the "dark" beginning stage of the transformation process.</note> known to none but a Christian follower of the Cabala <term>Cabalist Christian</term> <note>A practitioner of Christian Cabala, which combined Jewish mysticism with Christian theology to understand the secrets of nature and creation.</note>. 

We judge him to be the happiest of all physicians who knows this sovereign potion of our medicinal mercury, or the medicine of the Sun <note>In alchemical symbolism, the Sun represents gold and perfection.</note> of our *Aesculapius* <note>The Greek god of medicine and healing.</note>, which protects against the violence of death; for this, no better universal cure <term>Panacea</term> grows in any garden. But the great God does not reveal these "gifts of Solomon" <note>Refers to the legendary wisdom and wealth of King Solomon.</note> to everyone without distinction <note>original: "promiscuously"</note>. For it seems a wonder to most men when they see a created thing—through a hidden <term>Occult</term> implanted magnetic power <term>virtue</term> of its own kind—be brought into real activity.

As for example: the inborn <note>original: "ingenerated"</note> magnetic, potential power in iron drawn from the lodestone <term>Loadstone</term> <note>A naturally magnetized mineral (magnetite).</note>; in gold from mercury; in silver from copper; and so consequently...

<summary>Helvetius asserts that the transmutation of metals is a physical reality he has witnessed personally, describing the Philosopher's Stone as a divine gift and a universal medicine. He argues that such secrets are hidden from the masses and revealed only to the wise through the natural magnetic attraction between substances.</summary>

...frequently in all the metals, minerals, stones, herbs, and plants, etc.

But here I may rightly ask: Which of the wisest philosophers is so sharp-sighted <note>original: "acute"</note> that he can perceive by what means or overshadowing <note>original: "obumbration"</note> the imagination in a pregnant woman marks <note>original: "tinge"</note> her child with venomous or monstrous things? This process completes its work in a single moment if it is activated by any external object. I believe many will say it is a death-magical <note>original: "Morto-Magical"</note> or devilish work; but such babblers are afraid of the brilliance <note>original: "Resplendency"</note> of the essential light of truth, which painfully afflicts their owl-like eyes. 

But I may affirm that the stars are a cause of this matter, even though you or I perhaps cannot comprehend their heavenly influences. Nor should the plants which the earth provides be overlooked <note>original: "slighted"</note> in this regard, even though you or I cannot rightly judge the effects of their inherent <note>original: "ingenerated"</note> virtues from their external <term>signatures</term> <note>The "Doctrine of Signatures" was a historical theory that the physical appearance of a plant—its "signature"—indicated its medicinal use; for example, a walnut was thought to heal the brain because it looked like one.</note>. These plants clearly show those virtues, according to their degrees of power, in the healing and preserving of men's bodies. But are all men lacking in the light of understanding just because you or I lack the knowledge of how these powers—created for one and the same end—may be brought into activity? 

Thousands of such things might be cited as examples. Even if you do not know the splendor in the angels, the pure white <note>original: "candid"</note> brightness in the heavens, the transparency <note>original: "Perspicuity"</note> in the air, the clear clarity <note>original: "Limpidity"</note> in the waters, the variety of colors in the flowers, the hardness in stones and metals, the proportions in living creatures, the image of God in reborn <note>original: "regenerated"</note> men, faith in true believers, and reason in the soul—yet there is in them such a beauty which very few mortals have thoroughly perceived or plainly known.

It is a substance much brighter than the dawn <note>original: "*Aurora*"</note> or a glowing gemstone <term>carbuncle</term>, more splendid than the sun or gold, and more beautiful than the moon or silver. This most restorative <note>original: "recreating"</note> light can never be erased from my mind, even if it is not believed by learned fools or illiterate idiots <note>original: "asses"</note> who babble nothing but the superficial polish of arrogant, proud eloquence. All such "bats and brats" wander from the truth and, as time passes, vanish, miserably ensnared in their own errors. Yet our claim shall stand until the very end of all generations, being built upon the eternal foundation of triumphant truth. 

And although this art is not yet known to everyone, the <term>Adepts</term> <note>Those who have mastered the secrets of alchemy.</note> assert from experience that this natural mystery is only to be found with the great *Jehovah* <note>The Hebrew name for God.</note>, placed "Saturninely" <note>Associated with the planet Saturn, which in alchemy often represents the leaden, dark, or hidden beginning of the Great Work.</note> in the center of the world. In the meantime, we consider those happy who, with the help of art, are careful in how they "wash" this Philosophical Queen and circulate the universal <note>original: "catholic"</note> virgin earth within a magical, physical, crystalline device. Indeed, as *Khunrath* <note>original: "Khunrade"; referring to Heinrich Khunrath (c. 1560–1605), a famous German physician and Hermetic philosopher.</note> says, they alone shall see the Philosophers' King crowned with

...[built up] of all the colors of the world, emerging from his bedchamber and glass tomb <note>original: "glassy Sepulchre," likely referring to the alchemical flask.</note>, more than perfect in his external, glorified, fiery body. These observers shall see the salamander <note>In alchemy, the salamander represents the "fixed" substance that can endure the fire without being consumed.</note> casting out waters and using them to wash the "leprous" (imperfect) metals in the fire, just as I myself have seen.

What more can I say? These people shall see the depths <note>original: "Abyss"</note> of the alchemical <term>Spagyrick Art</term>, where this royal science lay hidden for so many years within the mineral kingdom as if in its safest embrace. Certainly, the true "Sons of this Art" <note>A common term for dedicated alchemists.</note> will not only reveal a river like the *Numicius* <note>original: "Numitius"; a river in Italy where Aeneas was said to have been purified.</note>—in which *Aeneas* was washed and freed from mortality by the command of *Venus*, instantly becoming like an immortal god—but they will also see the whole *Lydian* River (called *Pactolus*) turned into gold as soon as King *Midas* <note>original: "Mygdonian Mydas"</note> has washed himself in it. 

Furthermore, in a long sequence, they will see the bath of the naked goddess *Diana* and the fountain of *Narcissus*. Indeed, they will see *Scylla* walking in the sea without her clothes because of the intense <note>original: "fervent"</note> rays of the sun. They shall gather the blood of *Pyramis* and *Thisbe*, which turned white mulberries red. They will also see the blood of *Adonis*, transformed by the descending goddess *Venus* into the anemone rose; the blood of *Ajax*, from which sprang the beautiful hyacinth or violet; and the blood of the giants struck by *Jupiter’s* thunderbolt. They will see the tears of *Althaea*, shed when she took off her golden robes and laid them aside. They will see the drops from *Medea's* boiled water, from which green plants immediately sprouted out of the earth. They will even see *Medea's* potion, brewed from many herbs gathered three days before the full moon to heal her father-in-law <note>original: "her good old Father Jason"; in mythology, Medea used her magic to rejuvenate Jason's father, Aeson.</note>. Also...

Yet our assertion shall stand until the very end of all generations, being built upon the eternal foundation of triumphant truth. 

And although this art is not yet known to everyone, the experts <note>original: "Adept"</note> assert from experience that this natural mystery is only to be found with the great *Jehovah*, placed like Saturn <note>In alchemy, Saturn represents lead and the beginning of the "Great Work," but also depth, weight, and the center of the material world.</note> in the center of the world. In the meantime, we consider those people happy who, with the help of art, are careful in how they "wash" this Philosophical Queen and circulate the universal <note>original: "Catholick"</note> virgin earth within a magical, physical, and crystalline device <note>Likely referring to the glass alchemical vessel or "philosopher's egg."</note>.
