### Chapter 3: Concerning the third general Principle, etc.

...turns green and grows, and with its rising in the harvest season, shuts them again.

The Elements are threefold: namely, Spiritual, Soul-related <note>original: "Soul-ish," referring to the *anima* or the mediating force between spirit and matter.</note>, and Physical <note>original: "Bodily."</note>. There are three aspects of the Spiritual Elements: the physical aspect is the Heaven; the soul-related aspect is the Light; and the spiritual aspect is the Spirit. The soul-related elements are twofold: Wind and Air. The physical elements are also twofold: Water and Earth. One is always hidden within the other, and each one always contains the other six within it; one of them is always physically manifest, visible, and perceptible <note>original: "knowing," likely meaning 'known' or 'perceivable' in this context.</note> or tangible, while the other six remain hidden within it.

Each Element is also threefold within itself: spiritual, soul-related, and physical. The "spiritual earth" consists of precious stones or jewels, which serve as the body of the Spirit. The "soul-related earth" is gold. The "spiritual water" in physical form consists of pearls; the soul-related form is amber; and the physical form is coral.

In all of these dwell many powers, especially if they are made new and spiritual through water and spirit by means of Fire. All Elements, with all their powers, exist within the One, which is a spiritual Rock <note>The "spiritual Rock" often refers to the *Lapis Philosophorum* (Philosopher's Stone) or a Christological foundation in alchemical theology.</note>. Out of this Rock, the Water of Life springs for all creatures; it ebbs and flows throughout the whole world and fills everything in its elementary form. When this "One" is taken away from them at the end of the world, then all the elements will be consumed in and by fire.

# CHAPTER 6.

## Of the three special Principles: *Spirit, Wind, and Water.*

The three special Principles, insofar as they serve as foundational origins, arise from their own sources: the spirit comes from God, the wind from Nature, and the water from the rocks and wells of the Element. Every body in the material world <note>original: "Elementary world," referring to the realm composed of the four elements (earth, air, fire, water).</note> arises out of the water, including even Heaven and Earth. All life, soul, and breath in all bodies come from the wind, and all spirits come from the Spirit. The spirit possesses within itself the light and Heaven; the wind possesses the air; and the water possesses the earth. Now, just as everything has its origin, it is also fed and nourished by that same source, and it eventually returns there again.

<margin>Psalm 104:29, 30.</margin>
Water is air that has been gathered, concentrated, and made physically tangible. Air is wind that has been softly gathered into a perceptible, physical form. And the wind is a living, concentrated spirit. But the spirit is a "spoken word" <note>This refers to the *Logos* or the Divine Word used by God to create the universe.</note> which creates and brings a living thing into existence, so that it stands there substantially where previously there was only the word.

In the beginning of Creation, the Spirit moved over the water; by this "moving," we are to understand the wind, through which the Spirit breathed upon the waters and made them fertile for the creation of the world.

# CHAPTER 6.
## Of the third special Principle

All things that exist, move, and have life originate from the one, infinite, and eternal Father: *I AM* <note>original: "EHEVE," a transliteration of the Hebrew *Ehyeh*, the name God gave to Moses.</note>, *Jehovah*, and *Jesse* <note>original: "JESSE," likely used here as a name for God or a reference to the Root of Jesse/Jesus.</note>. He is the essential, self-existing, living Word, which is and was in the beginning and remains everlastingly. Compared to this Word, all other things are just nothing. Through it, all things are created, preserved, nourished, and fed in their spirit and life, as if through the breath of the Almighty. (4 Esdras 16:13; Hebrews 1:3; Matthew 4:4).

Now, by the three special Principles—spirit, wind, and water, which are also Elements—all Creation is finished. This is not only because they are the means by which the general Principles work, but also because they contain within them the proper seed of all things. This seed is found in the only true Element which we mentioned before. For these three carry in their body all "Salt" <note>In the alchemy of this period, "Salt" represents the principle of substance, stability, and the physical body.</note> and seed—whether fixed <note>Stable or solid.</note>, volatile <note>original: "flying," referring to spirits or gases that evaporate.</note>, or essential. They also contain Heaven and Earth, with all that is within them, and bring each thing forth into the world into its own proper substance.

<margin>X</margin> Just as everything is produced out of the special Principles according to the laws of Creation and birth, the fire—along with the curse and corruption—has made everything evil. Therefore, all that is born anew must return to water, wind, and spirit. Out of the spirit and spiritual water, one must be born anew by the wind into the image of the heavenly beings <note>original: "Cœlestials."</note>. This happens so that, in their glory, they are nothing other than angelic and divine, and bear the image of the heavenly.

<margin>X</margin> This new birth comes out of the "upper waters" and <margin>X</margin> out of a "heavenly earth," to speak in terms of the Elements, and they are nothing else but salt. There is another birth also that comes out of the fire, which is achieved through pain and torment. The new birth out of the water and through the water is achieved by drowning in water until death...

# Spirit, Wind, and Water.

Everything involving the earth occurs through death and corruption. Birth out of the fire, being a contrary element, occurs in and through the fire in Hell. Every new birth and regeneration—as well as every creation and change—is accomplished through the spirit.

The new birth out of water occurs when water is poured upon that which is to be reborn. This water drowns, kills, and reduces the bad, contrary elementary nature to nothing. Conversely, it stirs up the good, draws it out, and makes it glorious; it distinguishes the good from the bad, rejecting the bad while choosing and keeping the good.

The new birth out of earth occurs when a thing is reduced to its own proper earth, dies, and rots <note>original: "putrifieth"</note> within it. Afterward, it comes forth again and rises out of the earth with a new and spiritual body, separating from the wicked <note>original: "naughty," meaning worthless or evil in this period</note> and corrupted parts.

The new birth in the fire occurs when everything is cast into the fire, and whatever cannot withstand the fire is gradually consumed. Only that which is spiritual remains and is saved, and afterward, the new birth emerges with a spiritual body (1 Corinthians 3:13–15; 1 Peter 4:6 <note>These verses discuss the testing of one's work by fire and the judgment of the flesh so that the spirit may live.</note>). Although we speak here in physical <note>original: "Phyſically"</note> and elemental terms, men of understanding will judge these matters theologically, and the wise may investigate physically how everything has its own true Earth, Water, and Fire, and so observe this mystery.

Now, Spirit, Wind, and Water are the means by which all things in the world are brought about. The Word sends these forth to all creations, births, and changes. These are never still, for they are...

...with the Word, the **perpetual motion** <note>original: "Perpetuum Mobile," referring to a self-sustaining source of movement or energy</note>, as was mentioned above, and they cooperate continually within the Light, Heaven, Air, and Earth. These four Elements stand still and immovable, and into them the three special Principles overflow with their body, soul, and spirit—namely, the Water, Wind, and Spirit—and work out all things and finish them. The water is, as it were, the Element; the Wind is like the Angelic nature; and the Spirit is Divine.

# CHAPTER 7

## Concerning the specific principles: Body, Soul, and Spirit.

Within these, the Principles are enclosed and consist of a threefold Trinity; one always produces another, and they always stand in an orderly way within their subordinate parts. They agree together so that they make up a true and whole Harmony, and are enclosed at last in Light, Life, and Love. The body of all of them exists and consists of the water and also the Earth; the Water comes out of the Wind; the Wind out of the Spirit; and the Spirit out of God.

There is a threefold body: namely, a perceptible <note>original: "sensible," meaning able to be perceived by the senses</note> or touchable <note>original: "palpable"</note> body made of Water and Earth; a soulish <note>original: "Soulish," likely referring to the *psyche* or the animating life force that exists between the physical and the spiritual</note> body made of Wind and Air; and a spiritual body made of Heaven, Light, and Spirit. Thus, an elementary body, an angelic body, and a divine body are very clearly distinguished within man.

Furthermore, the Soul is corporeal <note>original: "corporeal," meaning having a physical or material nature</note> from the Air, from which it is also fed; it is "soulish" <note>original: "soulish," likely referring to the *psyche* or the animating life force between the physical and spiritual</note> from the Wind, and spiritual from the Light. The Spirit is corporeal from the Heaven, soulish from the Light, and spiritual from the spirit of the Elements, from Nature, and from God, depending on the nature of the creature. 

From these three general Principles, man also has a threefold spirit according to his measure and is the most perfect creature. One body always dwells within the other, and as soon as one body is dissolved and broken, at that same moment, another and more noble body is revealed—and this occurs in all things. If the body now becomes nobler, the Spirit must necessarily be more noble, high, and glorious. But this is the body which the wise seek: namely, the Salt <term>Salt</term> <note>In alchemy, Salt represents the principle of fixity, materiality, and the physical body that survives the fire</note> which contains everything within itself. 

This body is drowned in a Water which flows out of the center <note>original: "Centrum"</note> of the vegetables; it draws out all virtues <note>original: "vertues," meaning inherent powers or medicinal properties</note>, which afterward come together in a celestial spiritual body and provide that precious jewel <note>A common alchemical metaphor for the Philosopher's Stone or the perfected spiritual self</note>. All things that are killed and die naturally are drowned in a cold Saturnine water <term>Saturnine Water</term> <note>In alchemy, Saturn is associated with lead, coldness, and the slow process of decay or "mortification"</note>, for all natural death is caused by coldness. But whatever is killed in the Fire without Saturnine water is not prepared by Nature for a better state. 

We seek only the Salt in the fire, and then through the Water, and afterward cleanse and purify it with the baptism of Fire and Water. We should therefore fix our thoughts on the water and use the fire very carefully, because it is a contrary element before which nothing can survive except for the Salt.

This is the true body of all elements and of all things in the whole world; if that is taken away, then everything perishes quickly—even Gold itself in the fire. This is the true Heaven, where all powers dwell...

All living souls consist of water and blood within their seeds, formed through a moist warmth and a warm moisture, each within its mother. All growing things consist, in their seed, of a slimy water acting through the Salt, which is fixed in the root, moving <note>original: "flying," referring to the volatile or active state of a substance</note> in the herb, leaf, and grass, and essential in the flower; and all three concentrate at last in the seed.

All ***Minerals***, ***Metals***, and what belongs to them grow out of a rich <note>original: "fat," meaning fertile or nutrient-dense</note> earth, which the Salt of the earth hatches. They coagulate through a cold fire, which is a ***Saturnine Water***—that is, a fiery water and a watery fire that does not burn.

Just as all things upon the earth grow by rain and dew, it also rains, thaws, and is misty within the earth; thereby the minerals, metals, and the like grow. All of this comes from the Salt—whether fixed, moving, or essential. The moving Salt begets ***Sulfur***, and the essential Salt begets ***Mercury***.

Among the ***crawling creatures*** <note>original: "Vermin," used here in an older sense to describe reptiles or small land animals</note>, the chief is the ***Viper***, with her brood and kind, and she is ***Mercurial***.

Among the ***plants*** <note>original: "Vegetables"</note> is the ***Vine***, a channel from which three sorts of water come, as well as a noble ***mineral*** substance; it is the center of the plant world.

Among the ***minerals*** is ***Gold***—indeed, it is the Salt.

Of all these three, the concentrated center is man, who is above all else that is created.

## CHAPTER VIII.

### Of the Elements, and Contrary Elements
### in the Creation.

The seven elements or powers of the world rest only upon one, which is a true wellspring of all elements in our thoughts because it contains all things. An element is a thing from which, and in which, the world and everything else consists; without it, nothing can exist. Conversely, a "contrary element" is that by which the world and its elements are altered and corrupted, and by which they must at last completely perish.

Now all things are created out of three principles materially <note>original: *Materialiter*</note>: namely, out of the Word as a spiritual principle; out of nature as a soulish <note>relating to the soul or the animating force of life</note> principle; and out of the element and contrary elements as an incorporeal-corporeal <note>having the qualities of both spirit and matter</note> and corporeal-incorporeal principle. That is to say, things are made out of the elements according to "something," and out of the contrary elements according to "nothing."

<margin>+</margin> The Word is the "all," the elements are the "something," and the contrary elements are the "nothing." The "nothing" has become "something" by the Word of God; and the "something" will become "nothing" again when, at last, the Word is taken away. Although the contrary elements were once nothing, they are a principle on their own within the Creation because they were made—having not existed before—and are not counted by God as evil. <note>The text ends here but the catchword "evil" from the bottom of the original page completes the sentence.</note>

<summary>This chapter introduces the concept of elements and "contrary elements," explaining how the physical world is constructed from the divine Word (the "all") into matter (the "something") and its eventual dissolution (the "nothing").</summary>

<keywords>Elements, Contrary Elements, Creation, Three Principles, Materialiter, Word of God, spiritual vs. corporeal</keywords>

--- Page 202 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "contrary elements" from Chapter 8, explaining how they functioned before and after the Fall of Man.</meta>

# Chap. 8. Of the Elements,

...evil, but good, because God looked upon them and has covered and hidden their principle. This principle was once nothing, yet it was concentrated and fastened together in a physical <note>original: "corporeal"</note> form in and on the Tree of Knowledge of good and evil. This tree must have stood there as a witness—namely, to show that the world was created out of nothing. Yet this was meant to remain undiscovered and not "ashamed" (meaning it should not be broken), so that the shame of the whole world might not be exposed before the face of all the world, leading to confusion, death, and destruction.

<margin>+</margin>Now, as long as these contrary elements remained unknown and were not broken in their concentrated center, they were very good. But as soon as they are known in their depths, they become something that brings shame to the world, and at last, they altogether consume it and reduce it to nothing.

These contrary elements are three: darkness as physical <note>original: "corporeal"</note>, fire as soulish <note>relating to the soul or the animating force of life</note>, and corruption as spiritual. Yet, before the Fall, corruption was not "corruption" in the sense of decay, but only a process of alteration—not toward evil, but toward good—and a change and exchange of all created things. Before the Fall, fire was not consuming; rather, in its proper use, it was good and useful. In the same way, darkness was very good, serving as a rest and refreshment for all creatures. 

But now, darkness is a dwelling place for evil spirits. As far and high as the darkness in the air reaches, that is the extent of Satan’s dominion and reign (Ephesians 2:2; 6:12). Thus, through sin, these contrary elements have become the inheritance of Satan, who is the Prince of Darkness and the ruler <note>original: "potentate"</note> of the fire; the fire is over him, and he is the principle and the source of destruction <note>original: "perdition"</note>.

<meta>This page continues the discussion from Chapter 8 regarding the "contrary elements," explaining how darkness and fire, which were originally part of God's good creation, became the components of Hell and death following the Fall of Man.</meta>

# Chapter 8. Of the Elements,

Out of darkness, Hell is hatched, and it is threefold. 
1. **Physical** <note>original: "Corporeal"</note>, according to its location, beneath the earth in the lowest places (Ephesians 4:9, Luke 16:28, 1 Peter 3:19). 
2. **Soulish** <note>relating to the soul or the animating force of life; original: "Soulish"</note> in the reign of the Devil and the Prince of Darkness in high places. 
3. **Spiritual** in the Devil himself and his followers <note>original: "Children"</note>. This Hell will be cast at last into the lake of fire.

From the fire is produced an unquenchable, ever-burning sulfur <note>original: "Sulphur"</note>, and such a consuming fiery flame that kills life, yet always keeps death alive.

The fire of Hell is threefold: in Hell, in the Devil and his followers, and in the fiery pool. From this transformation—or rather, this destruction <note>original: "perdition"</note>—**Death** is finally born. In its physical form, Death is a cold fire and a fiery coldness. According to its soulish nature, it is a gnawing worm that continues forever; it devours but never eats, yet it always consumes and still generates itself again. According to the spirit, **Death** is essentially the Devil himself, who has fathered sin, just as sin has fathered him, the **Devil**.

Now, darkness was good before the Fall, for light was hidden within it—the light which God commanded to come forth out of the darkness. The fire was also good before the Fall because life rested within it; at that time, no flame was burning in the fire, nor was it visible. Change and transformation <note>original: "alteration"</note> were also good before the Fall because love revealed <note>original: "shew"</note> itself through them by the increase of the creatures. Since **Light**, **Life**, and **Love** include everything, they were also contained within these contrary elements. But they were separated from them, thrust out, and parted through sin. Thus, that which was very good became exceedingly bad, and turned good and evil into contrary and opposing things.

->And contrary Elements.<-

After the Fall, *Hell* and *Death* were brought forth <note>original: "begotten"</note>, and the fire was revealed and became visible and physical <note>original: "corporeal"</note>. Because of sin, this fire rose up into the heights; consequently, by God's permission and command, it can fall from heaven upon the wicked world. Specifically, it resides in and upon the fiery cherub before the entrance to Paradise, guarding the path to the Tree of Life. 

But at the glorious and joyful coming of the great God *Jesus Christ*, this fiery cherub must depart and descend, and all opposing elements must be reduced to nothing—that is, they must no longer reign. Furthermore, the Devil himself must be cast down from above, taken captive, and bound in prison for a thousand years. This will lead to the glorious liberty and redemption of all creation <note>original: "the Creature"</note> from futility <note>original: "Vanity"</note>, the curse, and death, where all things will be renewed to the state in which they were first created.

## CHAPTER 9

### Concerning the Principle or Origin of the Evil One and of the Angels.

Before God created this physical <note>original: "Elementary"</note> world, the angels, the angelic world, and Paradise—which existed above the "upper waters"—came first. This is as certain as the fact that nature existed before the physical elements, and God existed before all things. Therefore, lower things were always derived <note>original: "gotten"</note> from higher things, and the higher always exists before that which is below, just as the spirit comes before the soul and the body.

God called the angels through his Word out of the same light where he dwells, according to their spirit, for the purpose that they should serve him. He has presented them in fiery flame, according to their soul, and as a wind, according to their body. 

Because the angels originate from the light where God dwells, they can know through, in, and by that same light what God’s command is. This light is the face of God in Heaven and the spiritual food of the angels. The angels of children may behold this light, whereas, on the contrary, the angels of sinful men may not behold it until the sinner performs true re-

# Chapter 9: Of the Origin of the Evil One, etc. (Page 31)

...pentance, then his angel may appear again before that light and before the face of the Lord, which causes great joy among the other angels. But as long as the sinner does not repent, the Devil appears before God and accuses the sinner day and night before him.

Now, among God’s angels, *Lucifer* was the chief; for he carried the image of the clear morning star, which was and is the Son of God. But he was not content with that great honor and dignity; instead, he would gladly <note>original: "fain"</note> have been Lord and God himself, and no longer a servant. This covetousness was developed <note>original: "gotten"</note> in *Lucifer* through an ill look and eye toward God, becoming inflamed within himself in the same fiery flame from which the angels’ souls are made. This grew so intense and heavy that the light departed from *Lucifer’s* spirit, and in its place, an unspeakably great darkness came out of the fire which *Lucifer* himself had kindled; thus, instead of Heaven, it became a Hell itself.

So the fiery flame—unknown to *Lucifer*, undiscovered and hidden—was fanned by him out of envy and resentment <note>original: "grudgings"</note>, so that it turned into an essential anger; indeed, into a consuming fire. In this fire, life had at first resided, but it was afterward turned into a living death which never dies, and a deadly eternal life was manifested as a soul for *Satan*. At last, through *Lucifer’s* pride, a strange wind was generated <note>original: "gotten"</note> in *Lucifer* to serve as a body for him. *Satan* has completely lost the angelic principle <note>original: "Angelical Principle"</note> and self-existence, becoming instead a strange bird and a wild fly.

*Lucifer* tried to see if he could be a God, or like God—which he already was, in his own portion and measure. Therefore he is called a Tempter and *Satan*, and he became such a one: namely, both a God and a creator, as well as a creature of his own making, and he lost all

### Chapter 9: Of the Origin of the Evil One, etc. (Page 32)

...all of God's testimony entirely, as well as the testimony of good angels. He has been a knave or liar from the beginning because of sin, which gave birth to him <note>original: "begotten"</note>, and he has given birth to sin. He is the father of sin, and sin is his mother; she gave birth to him, and he to her, through covetousness in the leering eye of self-love and imagination.

Now, since sin is that evil which was discovered in its principle <note>original: "Principle"; here meaning its fundamental source or origin</note> by *Lucifer*, it has turned him into an evil one. Each is the source of the other, and so he cannot be excused by any means. Thus *Lucifer* has murdered himself and lost his angelic nature <note>original: "Angelical Principle"</note>; he is and remains a forlorn child and a son of perdition <note>A term from the Bible (John 17:12) referring to someone destined for spiritual ruin.</note>—the true *Antichrist* forever.

Thus, sin is acquired through coveting, and coveting through looking upon things, and looking upon things through imagination. This comes through self-love, which comes through an arrogant liberty; this comes through a false sense of security, and that through wantonness where there is no fear. For just as the fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom, so wantonness is the beginning of folly and sin. He who is fearful will not easily risk <note>original: "hazard upon"</note> sinning.

*Lucifer* was created by God as a good angel, and did so in such a way that he might easily have been kept from sinning. The same was true for *Man* if he had willed it. However, self-will brought him to that sin—indeed, his own wantonness. Yet, he could not be created so perfectly that it was impossible for him to fall into sin at all. The reason is that his "weight, measure, and number" <note>A reference to the Book of Wisdom 11:20, suggesting that created things are finite and limited compared to the infinite God.</note> could not endure it; he was not born directly of God's essence but had his own foundations <note>original: "Principles"</note> apart from God, though created through God. But whatever is born of God and of His seed cannot sin, because it is born of God, for whom it is impossible to commit sin.

# Concerning the Angels

Thus, the massive and abundant difference between the *Creation* (which was "very good" at the start) and the *Renovation* <note>original: "Renovation"; here referring to the spiritual restoration or "new birth" of a person</note> is made clear and manifest. This renovation was performed in and upon the "old creature" by the means and help of the Spirit of God. Among those born anew from God above—which is the only way to become children and heirs of God, and co-heirs with Christ—this truth remains unknown to the world and its "wise" children.

Now, the angels consist of *wind*, *fire*, and *light*. Because the fall of *Lucifer* stands before them as a warning, they cover their feet and faces before God with fear and trembling. They are humbled <note>original: "ashamed of themselves"</note> so that they may find grace before the *Lord God*.

Currently, they are a fiery flame for the protection of the godly, and a source of destruction <term>perdition</term> and death to the wicked. God is also a consuming fire within His angels—though not in or of Himself—and He will come with His angels and His *Power*, and with fiery flames for judgment.

### CHAPTER X.

->*Concerning the difference between light and darkness,*<-
->*as well as light and fire.*<-

Up until now, light was not counted among the Elements by the "wise" of the world, even though it was the first of them in the Creation; for in all creatures, the blood and eyes are formed first, not the heart. Now, light is an outpouring of God’s glory, and it never sets or decays in its spirit. It is a dwelling place for the seven spirits of God, just as darkness is a habitation for evil spirits. Within the light dwells the Spirit of the Lord: the spirit of wisdom and understanding, the spirit of counsel and of strength, the spirit of knowledge and of the fear of the Lord <note>A reference to the sevenfold gifts of the Holy Spirit found in Isaiah 11:2.</note>. All wisdom, understanding, and knowledge, all judgment and examination, and every truth and righteousness comes from and out of the light, and through the same.

And just as light illuminates the whole world and makes the day—indeed, is the day itself—so it also illuminates every spirit in all living souls. Just as there is no day without light, nor can there be, so no wisdom, knowledge, or understanding can exist without light. But light in its physical form <note>original: "body"</note>, in our opinion, is a pure, essential, spiritual Salt <term>spiritual Salt</term> <note>In the alchemical traditions of this period, "Salt" often represented the principle of physical substance or crystallization; here, the author suggests light is the fundamental "material" of the spiritual world.</note> from which all gems and preci-

## Chapter 10. Of the difference of light and darkness, etc.

...precious stones get their color, as do all flowers and beautiful things their beauty. The spirit of light works all of these things and adorns everything with beauty; for all colors are hidden within the light and are obtained from it. The soul of the light is nothing other than a joyful Life out of *Nature*, like an angelic life, and its spirit and eternal love proceed from the Spirit of the Lord.

Out of this light, God has made the heavenly lights <note>The Sun, Moon, and stars.</note>, which are solidified <note>original: "coagulated"</note>, living, and understandable lights. In their essence, they are nothing other than a spiritual, essential, and exceedingly pure crystalline salt <term>crystalline salt</term> <note>In the alchemical traditions of the 17th century, "salt" was one of the three primary principles of matter, representing solidity; here, the author describes the stars as a perfected, spiritualized form of matter.</note>. They are so highly refined <note>original: "tempered"</note> as anything can be, without any quality or property of heat, cold, warmth, moisture, or dryness, and they have their own motion within themselves. Specifically, the Sun always runs with the light and the day, but the Moon with the darkness and night, and the other stars move in their own places and order.

These heavenly lights always and everywhere clearly show the power of the Elements and the opposing Elements, and what their operations are. Through these powers, all things in the whole world are completed and crafted; the heavenly lights act much like a mirror <note>original: "Looking Glass"</note>, showing you exactly what is there.

Now, the powers of the Elements and opposing Elements change and rotate daily, weekly, monthly, and yearly. Because knowledge comes out of the light—searching out all the depths in every spirit, soul, and body—it presents these depths in the heavenly lights to prophesy, tell, and provide understanding to the observers, who are the Children of the Light. The spirit of light searches through all things and shows, by the heavenly lights, every ascendant <term>ascendant</term> <note>An astrological term referring to the planetary influences rising at a specific time, believed to govern the spirit or character of a being.</note> or spirit

# Chapter 10. Of the difference of light and darkness, etc.

...in all things, and also in humans—specifically, what spirit, soul, and body a person has received at birth <note>original: "nativity"</note>, according to the Elements and opposing Elements <term>opposing Elements</term> <note>These were the conflicting qualities (such as hot versus cold or wet versus dry) that were thought to balance or disrupt the human body and temperament.</note>, as well as which spirits find their center within him. If any of these influences are bad or evil, a person may decline or resist them through the holy and good spirit, just as a beast can shake off dust or flies.

Because every element has its own spirits—such as those of the Earth, the Water, and the Air—and because their eyes are more spiritual than our physical ones, they can see things within the lights of heaven and reveal them to humans. However, the children of light do not communicate with such spirits at all, nor should they, for it is only one step away from witchcraft.

Natural *magic* <note>In this period, "natural magic" was considered a legitimate science involving the study of the hidden (occult) properties of plants, minerals, and stars, distinct from demonic sorcery.</note> can accomplish much; the Wise Men of the East who came to Christ used it profitably. Yet, angelic magic can do even more, and divine magic more still. We must always strive for the best. Humans have within them a threefold *magnet* or *lodestone* <note>A natural magnet; used here as a metaphor for the human capacity to attract spiritual forces through desire and will.</note> by which they can draw all the spirits of the world to themselves and perform wonders. 

But what does the Lord say <note>original: "saith"</note> about it? See *Matthew 7:22* and *Luke 10:20* <note>These biblical passages caution that even those who perform miracles or cast out demons are not necessarily known by God; the focus should be on salvation rather than power.</note>. For by natural *magic*, demons may be cast out and great wonders performed. The Prince of Darkness can transform himself into the shape of ☞ an angel of light, and he tries to involve himself in every work. The present is a very dangerous time because all the spirits are stirring; their end is so near that it is hardly believed. The air is full of spirits, and the earth is also full of them, and every person...

### CHAPTER 11.

## *Of the Principle of Fire, and its Mystery.*

There is a *threefold fire*: namely, the fire of the *opposing element* <note>original: "contrary Element"</note>, the fire of the *angels*, and the fire of the *devils*.

The fire of the *opposing element* is *threefold*. *First*, before the Fall, it was a still, resting, and unmanifested fire, without a burning flame. *Secondly*, after the Fall, it became a kindled, manifested, burning, flaming fire. *Thirdly*, it is a cold, watery <note>original: "waterish"</note> fire which does not burn, yet it smokes and works into the earth upon *minerals* and *metals*. With this cold, watery fire, all things are compelled <note>original: "forced"</note>, as are the *metals*; for it calcines <term>calcinate: to reduce a substance to powder or ash by heat, or to oxidize it</term> them and turns them, as it were, into ashes, destroying and opening them.

This fire is primarily *threefold*: 

1. *Vegetable*, which is, so to speak, tempered; it is a well-refined <note>original: "rectified"</note> *vinegar*, which is extremely useful. Not many words are needed on this: *A word to the wise is sufficient* <note>original: "Sat sapienti dictum"</note>.

*Secondly*, it is a *mineral fire*, which is primarily the true *spirit of nitre* <term>spirit of nitre: a volatile or acidic spirit derived from saltpeter, often associated with nitric acid</term>, a spiritual water out of *saltpeter*, which has both heat and cold, and is both *hellish* <note>original: "Infernal"</note> and *heavenly* <note>original: "Cœlestial"</note>.

### CHAPTER 11.
## *Of Fire and its Mystery.*

<margin>+</margin>
Thirdly, it is *mercurial* or *saturnine* <note>associated with the planets Mercury and Saturn, or the metals mercury and lead</note>, a strong salt that has no equal. Without this, no metal is created <note>original: "engendered"</note>, broken down, or grown. Within this is hidden a great mystery, more than can be imagined. But the true spirit of *nitre* <term>nitre: saltpeter, a key ingredient in alchemy and medicine</term> must not be prepared without a "cold fire"; for the raging, horrible, and furious "hell" within saltpeter must be overwhelmed, drowned, and devoured by its own consuming fire, and thus be reduced to a blessed "heaven."

<margin>+</margin>
Now, when a "heavenly water" is available, then a new birth from above—out of water and spirit—can follow. Hidden here is a great medicine to be used in times of the raging plague, headaches, fevers, kidney stones <note>original: "the Stone"</note>, gout, and many other diseases. And truly, the time has come when all things must be made manifest. Although we have not yet prepared it with our own hands, the spirit of wisdom—which searches into all depths—can teach us everything secret and mystical. It can show, testify, and make known to us that which no eye has seen, nor ear has heard, and which has never entered into the heart of man.

<margin>+</margin>
Thirdly, there is also a metallic <note>original: "metalline"</note> cold fire which reduces all metals—even gold itself—to nothing. Only that noble "grain" <note>the essential "seed" or pure essence of the metal</note> hidden within the gold stays and remains, for it cannot be forced. That fire is lead, or *Saturn* <note>In alchemy, Saturn is the planetary name for lead</note>, which devours all metals and finally consumes itself in the fire as well. Just as a common fire consumes and devours wood and is itself eventually extinguished, a treasure <margin>+</margin> lies hidden in the remaining ashes <note>original: "cinders"</note>, which must be drawn out with hot water.

# Chapter 11. 
## Of Fire and its Mystery.

Metals have two kinds of waters: a cold one and a hot one, and both are fire. The cold water is *Saturn* <note>Lead</note>; the hot water is *Mercury*. Now, just as the one is an extreme, hellish cold, the other is an extreme, hellish heat. Because of this heat, it remains in a state of constant flowing, even though it feels cold to the touch.

In this "fire-water," metals—and especially gold—are born anew after their death. They are reborn in the metallic world and rule there; yet they are reborn as a clarified, crystalline, spiritual, and heavenly body. This body is so glorious that it can transform inferior and less precious metals into its own substance, provided they are of a similar nature.

It is of great value to know the nature of fire and its mystery. Without this knowledge, we cannot make any good or profitable use of it for our benefit. According to the progression of nature, everything must first be killed in the "cold fire"—as if passing through its own winter. It must die and rot <note>original: "putrifie"</note> if it is to be produced again in a new body.

Now, fire, according to its fundamental principle, is born out of darkness; it is produced from there and returns there again. But darkness was born out of the "Nothing." That "Nothing" stood there at the beginning of Creation in relation to the "Something" as a witness to what was created. For everything that is made and created was *nothing* before it existed. Before it was fashioned, it was not shaped without a frame, but was, as it were, a dark *Ens* <note>Latin: *Ens*, meaning "Being" or "Essence"</note> or "Being." Out of this, the Light is later born, which is a structured <margin>+</margin> <note>original: "fashionable"</note> being—that is, a visible thing made out of the invisible.

<summary>This page explores the dual nature of metallic "waters" (Saturn and Mercury) as transformative fires and explains the alchemical necessity of "death" and decay before a substance can be reborn in a perfected, heavenly state. It concludes by tracing the origin of all created things from a dark, invisible essence ("Ens") that emerged from the void.</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, fire, Saturn, Mercury, gold, transformation, rebirth, Ens, darkness, creation, metallurgy</keywords>

# Chapter 11. 
## Of Fire and its Mystery.

Therefore, darkness and fire—in a positive sense and as they existed before the Fall—are a byproduct <note>original: "excrement"; here meaning a residue or outgrowth produced during a creative process</note> of the Light. Yet they are good and useful, just like the material that a master craftsman <note>original: "Work-master"</note> hews or cuts away from the matter he intends to shape into some beautiful object. Just as chips of wood are of the same substance as the thing being framed from it, they are nevertheless a residue of it; and when these chips are thrown into the fire, they return to nothing. In the same way, once the opposing elements are recognized as separate, they are no longer good, but exist in opposition—hostile and completely worthless <note>original: "stark nought"</note>.

Furthermore, let it be known that in the divine world there are no opposing elements; indeed, there can be none in it, even though their power has pressed into it—even into the depths of God. Those depths grew dark when the Lord of Glory died on the **Cross**, and the fire of the raging wrath of God consumed Him, and death and perdition killed Him. Who can express this, or who knows what this saying truly means!

Also, in the angelic world there is no darkness, yet there is fire. This angelic fire is a byproduct <note>original: "Excretion"</note> of the light, from which the spirit of the angels is made. In the beginning, before the Fall, this fire was a quiet and hidden <note>original: "unknown"</note> fire and was very good, because it was and is the soul of the Light in the angels. But after the Fall, it was made manifest, known, and turned into a flame—one in which the raging anger of God rests, and in which all of God’s judgments consist and from which they emerge.

This fire now within the angels—with its rage, anger, and consuming flame—is not evil at all. Instead, it is a righteous fire of God’s justice to punish the wicked. All anger, rage, and judgment proceed out of the angelic world, as does the Law, which was proclaimed <note>original: "promulgated"</note> with fire. (Exodus 20; Acts 7:53, 38; Galatians 3:19; Hebrews 2:2).

## CHAPTER 12.

->*From what, within what, and by what all*<-
->*things, good and bad, exist, pass*<-
->*away, and yet how they last for*<-
->*ever.*<-

**A**lthough our knowledge and prophesying are only partial <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 13:9: "For we know in part, and we prophesy in part."</note>, we will not quench the spirit, and we are not to despise prophesying <note>A reference to 1 Thessalonians 5:19-20.</note>. The reader in the Lord should know that our wisdom—whether it concerns natural or spiritual matters—is learned from the Holy Scripture and not from secular <note>original: "profane"</note> writings. 

For the Bible is sufficient for us for all wisdom; in twenty-four years, we used no other book to find wisdom but the Bible. From this book, the spirit of wisdom, through the anointing <note>original: "anointing"; likely a reference to 1 John 2:27, referring to the internal teaching of the Holy Spirit</note>, can teach us all things, and it needs no other spirit or person to teach us.

In a word, everything exists <term>subsists</term> only through Salt. Things perish without it, and they perish within it, yet it lasts forever in both good and bad things.

<header>And yet how do they last forever?</header>

There is a threefold ***salt***, namely a ***Divine***, ***Angelical***, and ***Elementary*** salt. Everything must be seasoned with ***salt*** if it is to remain good, and ***salt*** is the most noble and healthiest balm, the best preservative and protector <note>original: "Conservative"</note>, and the highest strength. The ***salt*** of the ***Divine*** world is a ***true light***, a ***Spirit*** and ***Water*** from above, through which we are illuminated, breathed upon, and baptized—indeed, seasoned and salted—so that the power of hell may have no power over us. For everyone must be seasoned with fire, and must be tested with fire to see who and what survives <term>subsists</term> in it; that which survives is blessed, otherwise it is nothing at all. *Mark* 9:49–50; *1 Corinthians* 3:14 <note>The original text cites 1 Cor. 13:14, but the context of "trial by fire" refers to 1 Corinthians 3:13–15.</note>.

The salt of the ***Angelical*** world is a living <note>original: "quick"</note> life, concentrated by ***God*** into the Tree of Life. When that tree shall be opened <note>original: "broken"; likely referring to the sharing of its fruit or the revealing of its power</note> at the glorious coming of ***Jesus Christ***, then he will so gloriously and powerfully season all created things in Heaven and Earth—in this elementary world—with salt, that they shall be freed and redeemed from sin, ***Curse, Death, Devil, Vanity, Pain, and Misery***. That will be a noble food at that great ***Supper***, upon which all flesh—that is, all created things and every creature—shall eat, drink, and feed, as if upon an ***Angelical*** and ***Heavenly Manna*** <note>original: "Cælestial Manna"</note>. And then also shall the sharp, bitter, sour, consuming, devouring, perishing salt of the contrary element, which reduces things to nothing, be put away; it shall die and be banished to its own place. We have spoken already above concerning the elementary ***salt***.

There is another ***salt*** also of the contrary element, which is threefold: namely in the ***Earth***, ***Sea***, and ***Air***. The ***salt*** of the contrary element is a sharp, devouring, consuming ***salt***; it rules in all creatures and is always mixed with the good ***salt***. The good salt is held captive in this consuming, sharp ***salt***, and can never be set free until that which holds it captive—

It must be carefully and purely "killed" with a cold fire and be reduced to a new, noble birth.

It should be noted in passing that every ***lye*** <note>original: "Lee"; an alkaline solution</note>, especially that made from quicklime, is a cold fire. This same "fire," when applied to immature metals that still contain their ***sulfur***, ***mercury***, and ***arsenic***, may undoubtedly be of good use—especially in certain iron or copper mines in which the sulfur of ***Sol*** <note>original: "Sol"; the alchemical name for the Sun, representing gold</note>, the glory of gold, sufficiently appears. For that which is volatile <note>original: "flying"</note> must be made firm and glorious through that which is fixed. And truly, there is more hidden here than the world believes.

Now, in ***salt*** there is both ***life*** and ***death***. And just as good things have their ***salt***, so do bad things, and both are firm. The good is firm because life is within it; the bad is also firm because there is both ***life*** and ***death*** within it. For what death kills with extreme...

<header>And yet how they last for ever?</header>

[...coldness,] <note>The text begins mid-sentence, continuing from the previous page's discussion on the properties of fire and cold.</note> so that life revives again by fire. Therefore, the fire is the life within the Devil and his children, and the cold is *death*. But it is a kind of life that is not of God, nor does it come from light and love. Instead, it comes from the Devil himself—out of darkness and anger—and is accompanied by unspeakable torment, anguish, pain, and misery.

The salt in the sea is a foolish *salt*, raging and furious, about which much could be said because a *mystery* is hidden within it. It also shines in its own fire and is a *sulfurous light* <note>original: "Sulphurious light"</note> that can be seen.

Finally, the salt in the air is essential and arsenical, and it poisons things on Earth: people, animals, and fruits, etc. These three types of *salt* belonging to the contrary element <term>contrary element</term><note>In alchemical philosophy, this refers to a corrupted or destructive version of a natural element that opposes the life-giving order of God, often associated with the state of nature after the Fall.</note> were revealed after the Fall of Man. Therefore, we must separate *salt* from *salt*—that is, the good from the bad—to reject what is evil and embrace what is good. In the end, all bad things fall to the Devil’s share, especially the contrary elements and their *salt*. These make up the fiery pools that devour and gnaw, yet do not consume; they exist forever, yet are not truly alive but dead—dying forever, and yet never living. Thus, it is rightly named a "contrary element."

# CHAPTER 13.

## *On the Creation of the World.*

Now that we have understood the *principles* within their *subordinate* forms, and the *center* concentrated both within and upon the *elements* (as well as in the *contrary elements*), we can observe how all things are created by the *Word*. We must also understand what *Shamayim* <note>original: "Scha Ma Jm"; the Hebrew word for heavens: שָׁמַיִם</note> is, as it is written in the book of Genesis <note>original: "book of the Creation"</note>. It says that God, *Elohim* <note>original: "Elohim"; a Hebrew name for God: אֱלֹהִים</note>, created the *Shamayim* in the beginning. Translators have rendered this as *heaven* and *earth*, but this runs contrary to the text and against the actual *order* of creation. Who can tell us what *Shamayim* properly is? It is true that we do not speak in *tongues* <note>In this context, the author refers to the spiritual gift of speaking in unknown languages (1 Corinthians 12:10), contrasting it with his own focus on interpreting scripture.</note>, for we are more occupied with *prophesying* <note>In the 17th-century sense, "prophesying" meant the inspired interpretation and explanation of the Bible.</note> according to the grace bestowed upon us. Therefore, we will offer the interpretation of it to others, allowing them to search and learn what *Shamayim* means.

What is known is that all other things came out of *Shamayim*, including the *water* from which *heaven* and *earth* were *created* in the beginning of the creation. It is not enough for us to know that the world was *created* out of *water*; we must also ask where that *water* came from, out of which the world was made. For the wise pursue *wisdom* even to her greatest depths, and do not give up... <note>The text continues on the next page, following the catchword "over" which completes the phrase "give not over," meaning "do not give up."</note>

<summary>The author begins an investigation into the biblical creation story, arguing that traditional translations of the Hebrew word "Shamayim" as simply "Heaven and Earth" are inaccurate and hide deeper truths about the primordial water from which the world was formed.</summary>
<keywords>Creation, Shamayim, Elohim, Genesis, primordial water, elements, biblical translation, Hebrew, cosmology</keywords>

...over, until they find the bottom and all the foundational *principles*.

The Book of *Wisdom* says <note>Wisdom of Solomon 11:22. The author uses "moment" in the sense of the smallest movement or weight, and the "tongue" refers to the needle of a balance scale.</note> (*"The whole world is before you, O God, like a tiny movement of the needle on a pair of scales, and like a drop of dew that falls in the morning upon the earth."*)

It is manifest that all *water* and *dew*, before they become physical *water*, are first a *vapor*. But we must investigate how, from what, from where, and by what means that *vapor* arises. In our opinion, it is nothing other than a physical spirit that encloses everything within itself, yields and gives everything to all things, and at last gathers everything back to itself. The very substance out of which the world is *created* is also within all things; without it, there is nothing anywhere. When that substance is taken away from a thing, it can no longer exist.

To explain the depths of the creation from which the world was produced, we find they are threefold. First is the *Word* of *God* in which there is *Light*, through which all things were made. That which exists in all things acts as the spirit, according to the highest degree in the *creation* of the world; and this is the true spiritual *seed* of all things, without which nothing exists or can exist.

Secondly, there is the *Soul* of the whole world, which is distinguished from *God* just as the *breath* is distinguished from the *spirit*. It is the breath of the speaking *Word* of *God*. In place of the soul, it is the true life of all things according to the highest degree, and it is the *soulish sustenance* <note>original: "soulish feed"; this likely refers to a "soul-seed" or the nourishing life-force of the world.</note> of all things in the world.

Lastly, the *salt* is the body and physical sustenance <note>original: "bodily feed"</note> of all things and of the whole world, in which the *Word* and the *Spirit* of *God* dwell and rest.

These three hatch from themselves a *water*, which is *Shamayim* <note>original: "Schamajim"; the Hebrew word for "heavens": שָׁמַיִם. In this context, it refers to a primordial, celestial water.</note>, out of which the whole world has its...

### Chap. 13. Of the Creation of the World.

Its *origin* <note>original: "original"</note> belongs to the middle degree, but the lower degrees are the elements. These three provide and establish the three general principles for the creation: *God*, *Nature*, and the *Elements*. Again, these three are the *Spirit*, *Wind*, and *Water*; and finally, every creature is enclosed within these three, and everything is totally contained within *Light, Life, and Love*.

The *Word* is *God*, and *God* is the *Word*. The *Spirit* is *Nature*, an *out-breathed breath* of *God*; and *Nature* is the *Spirit* and *Soul* of the *World*. The *Salt* <note>In alchemical philosophy, "Salt" represents the principle of body, solidity, and crystallization, rather than just common table salt.</note> is an element of all elements, and the elements in their glory are nothing other than a Salt, *salt* <note>original: ἁλς (als)</note>. From these three consists the *Heavens* <note>original: SchaMajm (Shamayim)</note> and the whole creation of the world, in each and all of their true principles. In all creations, the *Word* is the beginning, the *Spirit* the middle, and the *Salt* is the end.

In the beginning of the creation, the *Heavens* <note>original: SchaMajm</note> were unfashioned and unframed; there was also *darkness* over the face of the deep, and there was a *Chaos* or confused state of being. But the *Spirit* of the Lord moved upon the water, and thereby it became *seminal* <note>original: "seedy"; meaning it was filled with the seeds or potential for life</note>. The first thing created in it was *Light*, but it was contained within the *darkness* for a long time, until God said, *"Let there be light"* <note>original: Lehior (Yehi Or)</note>: "Come forth, you light, and come before the day, and make a day, so that it may be *Light*." And presently *light* parted from the darkness. According to its body and being, light is an essential, most refined spiritual *salt*, which cannot be perceived <note>original: "brought to the sense"</note> except through the eye.

<margin>+</margin>The *darkness* contains within it *Fire*. The light was parted from *darkness*, and the fire lay secretly hidden within it, which afterward broke forth to be visible by reason of sin; it is called not *Or* (*Light*), but *Ur* (*Fire*) <note>The author distinguishes between *Or* (the Hebrew word for light) and *Ur* (the Hebrew word for fire or flame).</note>.

After the *Light* was created, the *Heaven* was made: a firmament out of the *water*, like *ice* and *crystal*.

### Chap. 13. Of the Creation of the World.

In this state, the flying, volatile <note>original: "foulish"; likely meaning "fowl-like" or volatile, as birds were often used in alchemy to represent volatile spirits</note> salt of life became fixed and firm. *Heaven* itself is such a salt, in which all the powers of life and of the soul dwell; from there, they are poured forth from above into the lower <note>original: "nether"</note> world through the *Spirit, Wind, and Air*. The body of these elements is the water, into which the flying salt is carried.

<margin>*Angelical world*</margin>
After *Heaven* was created, the *Earth*—the physical center of the world—was formed. It is a dense <note>original: "gross"</note> body that contains the *fixed salt*, in which all the elements of the earth converge <note>original: "incenter"</note>. The light is compared to the divine world, the *Heaven* to the *Angelical* <note>original: "\*English"; almost certainly a typo or OCR error for "Angelical," matching the marginal note and the traditional hierarchy of Divine, Angelical, and Elementary worlds</note> world, and the earth to the elementary world.

Above the *Heaven* and the *Firmament* are the spiritual realms, above the waters into which nature pours itself. These spiritual realms have their own world above the "upper waters" and are the true *Paradise*, where there is pure <note>original: "meer"</note> light and no darkness. This world is being made manifest in these last times, in which the *Wedding <note>original: "Nuptial"</note> of the Lamb and his Bride* and the great, true *Supper* will be held. Those who have a part in the first *Resurrection* and *Transformation* <note>original: "Change"</note> at the coming of *Jesus Christ* shall meet the Lord and be taken up into the air to go with him into *Paradise*, and shall thus be with the Lord always. The whole sphere of the world of earth and water is carried and held up by the air, just as a body is kept and held up by the spirit and breath so that it does not fall.

# CHAP. XIV.

->*Of the Specific Creation* <note>original: "particular Creation"</note><-

The Creation in its order is threefold. First, it is *General* in the *Heavens* <note>original Hebrew: *SchaMajim* (Shamayim)</note>, which was the first *Matter* <note>original Latin: *Materia*</note>, and remains so; it is that out of which all things are corporally created, and into which all principles come together and are concentrated. Afterward, it is *Special*, occurring on and in the *Elements*, such as the *Lights* (the sun and moon), *Heaven*, *Earth*, and *Water*. The *Spirit*, *Wind*, and *Air* are contained within the *Heavens* <note>original: *SchaMajim*</note>. Lastly, it is *Specific* <note>original: "Particular"</note>, as seen in all those things which were created, produced, and made out of, in, and by the *Elements*.

The first things that were made in the specific creation were the *Vegetables* <note>original: "Vegitables"</note>—all growing things on earth, such as *Grass*, *Herbs*, and *Trees*, among which the *Vine* <note>original: "Vine-Tree"</note> is the chief. Now every thing has its proper seed within itself; therefore, at this point *Creation* ends, and *Conception* and *birth* begin, occurring out of, in, and by its own seed. But at first, all things—grass, trees, and herbs—were brought forth out of the earth through the Word, Spirit, and Salt. The *Salt* has given to the grass, herbs, and trees their bodies, which they all have within them. The *Spirit* has given them power and virtue, especially for *Medicine* <note>original: "Physick"</note>; but the Word gives the blessing to it. The true medicine <note>original: "Physick"</note>, virtue, and blessing may be sought and gathered out of the salt of the

<page-num>24</page-num>

## Chapter XV.

### Of the Mystery of the Word.

It is known from the Holy Scriptures that all things are made and created by the Word, and that all things continue to be made and are preserved through the Word. But here we will not speak *theologically*, but only naturally in accordance with the Creation; neither do we speak *theosophically* <note>original: "Theosophically"; referring to a form of philosophical or religious thought based on a mystical insight into the nature of God</note> of the depths of God, both of which we save for another time and place.

The Word of Creation is the general power of God, out of which, in which, and by which all things exist, are sustained, and will be. Every creature makes use of this general power of Almighty God for its own best good; but only man abuses it, as do the devils and spirits by God's permission. From this it follows that men must give an account of every idle word, because they have abused the breath of the Almighty and used it to sin. Oh, that I could put a lock on my mouth, so that I might not sin <note>original: "transgress"</note> with my tongue!

Because all mysteries are now hidden in the Word, the Word also performs all things in the world. Therefore, we say that from this arises a threefold magic <note>original: "Magick"; in this period, "magic" often referred to the study of the hidden or "occult" forces of nature and the spiritual realm</note> through faith—that is, a power to know something in order to bring it to an effect.

<summary>This chapter introduces the "Word" as the universal power of God that creates and sustains all things, while warning that humans are uniquely accountable for abusing this power through sinful speech.</summary>
<keywords>The Word, Creation, theology, theosophy, universal power, sin, magic, faith, speech</keywords>

--- Page 230 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Word" from Chapter XV, specifically exploring how it relates to "Natural Magic" and the influence of the human and divine spirit over astrological forces.</meta>

<page-num>55</page-num>
->Of the Mystery of the Word.<-

First, there is a *Natural Magic*, which comes out of *Natural Faith*, in which there is such a *Magnet* or *lodestone* that it can draw all things to it. This faith is acquired in *Man*, either from nature within his Spirit—which is the true and right ascendant <note>The "ascendant" in astrology is the sign rising on the eastern horizon, thought to govern a person's life; here, the author argues the human spirit is more powerful than any star</note>, namely the spirit of man, and by no means this or that *Star* <note>original: "Astre"</note> or *Constellation*, as the ignorant imagine—which acts only as a sign of the rising power <note>original: "in signum ascendentis"; as a sign of the ascendant</note>, etc. Or, this faith is worked in man through the art and instruction of *natural magic*, so that his spirit receives the ascendant and rejoices in it. The ascendants in *Man* may very well be transformed, transplanted, and altered by the spirits.

The wise men of the *East* made lawful use of this *natural magic*—without *witchcraft*—for they knew the *Star* and advanced so far in this art that they not only gained great knowledge but also performed wonders.

This *magical art* is learned to a true and perfect degree from the *Spirit of God*, and it goes before and beyond the ascendant, because other *spirits* commonly mingle with it. *Daniel* and his companions had studied this *magical art*, as had *Moses* and the *Prophets*; they far exceeded the skill of the wise men and *magicians* of *Egypt*.

False *magicians* took their art from this *natural magic*, but because the true ascendant—namely, the *Spirit of God*—was not within them, but they only had their *elementary* power or their *master's* ascendant, *Lucifer* appeared to them as an *Angel* of *Light*. He became the rising power within them and turned them into *sorcerers*. Now, just as true *magicians* know and perform all things through the *Word of God*—which speaks in them and operates through them—so the *sorcerers* abuse the power of the Word and perform wonders with it, until the staff of *Moses* and <note>original: "Moses his staff"; a reference to Exodus 7, where Moses's staff turned into a serpent and swallowed the serpents of the Egyptian magicians</note>

<meta>catchword: Serpent</meta>

*Balaam* was a true <note>original: "right"</note> natural magician; but the spirit of greed <note>original: "covetous spirit"</note> was dominant <note>original: "ascendent"</note> in him, so that he went to the sorcerers—that is, to the spirit of sorcery. The spirit of greed <note>original: "Avarice"</note> had blinded his eyes, so that he could not see the angel that resisted him, but the donkey <note>original: "Ass"</note> saw him and was afraid <note>original: "shye"</note>. Therefore, *Balaam* had to be killed by the sword, as others who deserved it. (*Numbers 31:8*).

From this false *magical* art come all *sorcerers* and *witchcrafts*, with their bewitching spells, signs <note>original: "tokens"</note>, words, and works, and all those who have familiarity with spirits. Let everyone beware of spirits, and let them not rejoice when spirits draw near to them; but rather flee from them and pray to the Father of *Lights* for the *Holy Spirit*, so that He may come to them.

Second, there is a prophetical and apostolic magical art, which comes out of faith in God's Spirit within His children, in whom the Word dwells with glory. This Spirit speaks to them and in them, as we read in the Prophets: "*The Lord has spoken to me*." Specifically, this is not always outwardly with a loud voice, but rather inwardly. This is how old *Simeon* received an answer from the Lord; this is also how the Lord spoke through the Ephod <note>An ornamental vestment worn by the Jewish high priest, often used for seeking divine guidance</note>, and so on. By this magical art, the Prophets and *Apostles* performed many miracles and raised the dead, and they did so only by the Word.

The Devil presumes to imitate this magical art—namely, by making it seem as if the word speaks out of *crystals* (by looking into them), or out of rings in which a spirit perhaps dwells and speaks, and so on. But this is not the Word; it is only a spirit trapped <note>original: "bewitched"</note> within it.

Finally, there is an even higher *magic* belonging to God's children, which works above and beyond nature through faith. Examples of this include when *Moses* divided the waters with his staff <note>original: "Rod"</note>, and when *Joshua* commanded the sun and moon to stand still.

But in the renewed future world, all these things will be clear and manifest to the praise of God.

# CHAPTER XVI.

## Concerning the Mystery of the Created, Lower, Visible Things.

God has brought forth all created, visible, earthly <term>sublunary: literally "under the moon," referring to the physical world of change and decay</term> things out of the invisible things that existed at the beginning. He made them into something tangible and visible, and gave to each of them a *Body*, *Soul*, and *Spirit* according to their kind. In them, He has hidden His invisible glory—that is, the invisible within the visible, and the *celestial* <note>original: "Cœlestial"</note> within the *terrestrial* <note>original: "Terrestrial"</note>. This is the mystery which we must diligently seek: the hidden *wisdom* which no eye has seen, nor can see, nor has any ear heard, nor has it entered into the heart of man.

All these earthly created visible things have a *terrestrial* body, which is visible. But they also have a *celestial* body hidden within them. This body remains invisible until the visible body is dissolved and broken; afterward, the invisible body is revealed. This body is heavenly and *spiritual*, consisting of water and spirit, and is nothing other than a crystalline—indeed, a newborn—salt of life, which cannot be overcome by any opposing element.

Furthermore, every thing has a *soulish life* <note>The author uses "soulish" to describe the natural, animal life that sustains physical bodies through breathing</note>. This is the kind of life that must breathe the common air and is nourished by it—a life that exists only for a moment and must be mortal, so that nothing of it lasts. Now, within this *soulish life* is a quickening Spirit, which does not breathe, as 

<summary>This chapter explains that all physical things contain a hidden, celestial reality within their terrestrial forms. Every created thing possesses a visible body and a mortal "soulish" life, but also an inner spiritual body and a "quickening Spirit" that survives physical dissolution.</summary>
<keywords>sublunary, celestial, terrestrial, body soul and spirit, salt of life, crystalline, soulish life, quickening spirit</keywords>

<page-num>59</page-num>

*soulish life* <note>The author uses "soulish" to describe the natural, animal life that sustains physical bodies through breathing</note>; but it has life and is in itself a Spirit of life, and not merely a breath. It has eternal life within it and is nothing other than the Spirit of God and the breath of the Almighty that gives life to all. Finally, all things have a spirit that returns to the place from which it came and does not remain in the dead; because it is not the spirit of the dead, but of the living. It is the Spirit of God, which does not remain forever in or through the old creation and creature, but only in and through the *new* creation, which is from above. Thus, nothing in this world is lasting except for *vanity* and *corruption*, yet it shows us clearly how all these created, sublunary <term>sublunary: literally "under the moon," referring to the physical world of change and decay</term> visible things are an image of the things above.

God has revealed this mystery to His children and to the wise: namely, that this lower created, visible, elementary world is an image of the upper visible spiritual, celestial <note>original: "Cœlestial"</note>, and even divine world. Therefore, when the visible elementary world vanishes, then the spiritual world—which is currently invisible—will be made manifest and visible. Thus, there is no creature that does not show the mystery of the superior spiritual world. The mysteries and wonders of this spiritual world will be preached in the future renewed world in *Zion*.

Now the Apostle says clearly:
> *We do not look at the things that are visible, but at the things that are invisible* (2 Corinthians 4:18).
> *Seek the things that are above, and not the things on Earth* (Colossians 3:2).
> *In my Father's house are many dwellings that last forever*, says Christ (John 14).

Why should we regard the visible things, which are fading away? The Apostle says:
> *If there is a soulish body, then there is a spiritual body also* (1 Corinthians 15:44).
> *And when this house of our earthly tabernacle <term>tabernacle: a temporary dwelling, here referring to the human body</term> is broken, then we have a house from above from God, which...*

### Chapter 16: Concerning Created Visible Things

Now, just as **God** continually pours His invisible glory down into this sublunary <term>sublunary: literally "under the moon," referring to the physical world of change and decay</term> world, He also gathers it back to Himself. Then, when everything reaches its conclusion at the end, He will set them before Him in a new creation, as it is written in *Revelation 21:5: "Behold, I make (or create) all things new."*

But before this new creation arrives, the renewal of the old creation and its creatures must come first. Namely, this happens in the joyful coming of the **Lord**, which will be with great power and glory, because everything called a "creature" <note>The author uses "creature" here to mean any created thing or being in nature</note> shall be set free (Romans 8:23). Freed from the **Devil, the Curse, and Death**, there will then be a joyful *Jubilee* <term>Jubilee: a season or occasion of great rejoicing or liberation</term>.

Now we must understand that there will be a great difference between the "renewing" and the "new being" itself. The old creature is made new within its old way of being; but the new creature has a new essence <note>original: "essence"</note>, and that does not come from below, like the old, but from above. For the true essence is found above, while what is below is only a type and an image; this is the mystery we are to observe. The true principles and elements are above; those here below are only a shadow. Below are merely terrestrial bodies, but above are the celestial ones, although they are hidden within those below.

The terrestrial bodies are mere **ashes**, but the **celestial** ones are a noble salt of life. Terrestrial life is only foolishness and a mouthful of breath; if that breath is gone, then everything falls down. But the celestial life is an **eternal life** and cannot die. The terrestrial spirit is but a wind; if that is gone, it flies into the air and

This is the great and miraculous mystery which you—O man, O Adam, O you image of God—should observe above all else: that you may know yourself in God, and God in you. You should seek to know and learn what man is, what Adam is, and what the "Image of God" is—that is, what you yourself are (which we discuss in our book, *Adam*). This is the greatest wisdom: namely, for a person to know themselves through a perfect and true knowledge, which is spirit and truth. He who does not value this but despises it—despising what is, in fact, God's image—rebukes himself and will also be rebuked by God.

# Chapter 17.

## *Concerning the Creation of Man and His Anatomy.*

A person has nothing so important to care for as themselves, so that they may know their own best interest and salvation. Now, one who truly knows themselves in spirit and in truth also knows **God** and all things. Therefore, self-knowledge is of the greatest benefit to a person. To speak briefly, all things—including humans—consist of *one, three,* and *seven*. The "one" is the individual, subsisting by itself. The "three" are the Body, Soul, and Spirit. And the "seven" are primarily the *seven* Powers, according to the *seven* Powers of the *seven* Elements and the *seven* Spirits of **God**. Every creature possesses these *seven* powers within itself in its glory.

Even so, *man* is a single individual personally—subsisting individually <note>original: "υφισάμενος individualiter"</note>—in his own self-subsistence. But he is composed of three parts—Body, Soul, and Spirit—and is evidenced by seven Powers:
1. by *Movement*,
2. by *Hearing*,
3. by *Seeing*,
4. by *Smelling*,
5. by *Tasting*,
6. by *Thinking* and *Reasoning*,
7. by *Sounding* or *Voice*.
*Movement* contains life; to feel, see, walk, and so on, are all considered as one category.

In this respect, man is *physical* <term>original: "Soulish"; here referring to the natural or biological life (Greek: psychikos) shared with animals</term>, like the living souls and beasts, which possess all these qualities but only in their own portion, measure, number, and weight—specifically, as much as belongs to them. In this regard, humans have no more than the animals do; they share with them a living soul, derived from and following the Elements, having the same beginning from the earth and the same return to the earth.

In this way, he is very distinct from the beasts; indeed, he is even above the angels. The Kingdom of God is hidden within this heavenly soul, and the true essence of humanity consists of this breath of the Almighty, by which he becomes a truly immortal man. *But in his other, elemental part, he is like the beasts: earthly, corruptible, mortal, dust, and ashes.*

## CHAPTER XVIII.

<margin>X</margin>
### Concerning the Image of God, in Which Man is Created

That man is created after God’s image is manifest from Scripture (*Genesis 1:26–27; James 3:9*). But the only question is this: what is this "Image" after which *Man* is created? Every creature or beast is made according to its own kind and image, but Man alone is made after God’s image. This Image is Christ, who is *truly and bodily the radiance* <note>original Greek: "ὄντως καὶ σωματικός ὃς ὢν' ἀπαύγασμα"</note>; he is essentially and bodily the Image of the invisible God (*Colossians 1:15*). 

For God is a Spirit, and may not be felt or seen, unless it be in His essential substance and substantial essence. Just as a man—in his true humanity <note>original: "Manhood"</note>, by which he is distinguished from all other living souls—is a spirit or spiritual being within his spiritual soul, yet a man's spirit cannot be seen, felt, or known except in his body, in which he dwells with all his fullness. In the same way, the invisible God, who is a Spirit, cannot be seen or known except in *Christ* and His substantial body, in which God the Father...

<header>Chapter 18. Of the Image of God,</header>
<meta>catchword: Now</meta>

the *Word* and the *Holy Spirit* <note>original: "Holy Ghost"</note>, together with the whole fullness of the Godhead, dwell bodily (*John 14:9; 1 John 1; Colossians 2:9*). And this is the Spiritual *Adam*, a life-giving <note>original: "quickening"</note> Spirit, the Heavenly Man, the Lord from Heaven (*1 Corinthians 15:45, 47*).

The Image of God is threefold; namely: 

1. The Image of the Essence essentially: just as a man is his own image in his own proper essence and body, so *Christ* also is the essential and bodily Image in God. Just as a man's body is in a man, so is *God* in *Christ*: as a man is in his body, his own essential, self-subsisting, bodily, and personal Image. 
2. The Image of that form, or the outward appearance of that form, face, and members. 
3. The Image of the living properties, power, or by whatever name it may be called. 

*Here* the mystery of the Image of God is made manifest: that *God* in *Christ*, and *Christ* in *God*, was a Man much sooner than we were; for we are fashioned after him in all things, and are thus his *representation* <note>original: "counterfeit"; in the 17th century, this often meant a portrait or a direct likeness rather than a fraudulent fake</note>.

*Christ*, the Image of God, and man, who is made out of God’s image and honor, are distinguished in this way; namely: 

1. As the Image and the Essence. 
2. As Essence and Essence. 
3. As Spiritual, Heavenly, and Divine, versus Soul-oriented <note>original: "Soulish"; likely a translation of the Greek *psychikos*, referring to the natural, earthly life of the soul as opposed to the spirit</note>, Terrestrial, and from below—that is, as *Adam* and *Adam*, Man and Man, and as that which is above and that which is below. 

The *Soul-oriented Adam* is not an Image of God according to the essence, as *Christ*—the *Reason* <note>original: "Reaſon"; likely referring to the *Logos* or Divine Word</note>—is, because his essence is terrestrial and from below. Instead, that image exists only in and upon that terrestrial body as a representation, and that in a terrestrial, soul-oriented essence from below, out of the earth. Thus, the *Soul-oriented Adam* and terrestrial man is an image of the Spiritual *Adam* and Heavenly man, just as a stony, wooden, or wax image of a living man is not made of human essence (the flesh and blood), but of another being entirely.

<summary>This page defines the "Image of God" as a threefold reality centered on Christ, the "Spiritual Adam," while describing earthly humanity as a "soulish" or terrestrial reflection of that divine original.</summary>
