<header>After which man is created.</header>

Just as essence and essence are distinct from one another, so it is with frame and frame. 

1. The inward form of God is the most holy Godhead, which dwells bodily in Christ in all its fullness. From this form, man has received the breath of the Almighty in a heavenly soul, becoming his inward essential form and true manhood.

2. The bodily, visible, palpable, and personal form of <margin>+</margin> God—the essence in which God personally appeared <margin>+</margin> and was personally made manifest—is the flesh of the Word, the body of Christ. Following this pattern, man has a body of flesh, blood, and bones; however, this is not divine, spiritual, or heavenly, but is instead "soulish" <note>original: "Soulish"; likely referring to the *psyche* or natural life (1 Corinthians 15:44-46), as opposed to the spirit</note>, earthly, and from below.

3. The manifest face—the person <note>original: "πρόσωπον" (prosopon); Greek for face, mask, or personhood</note>—and the looks and presence on the head, body, and all members, along with their powers and strength. Man is also framed after this and is to be viewed in this way. Many mysteries may be observed in this consideration, even though the "spirit of error" claims that God has no head, eyes, ears, face, nose, mouth, hands, or feet because he is a spirit. This description actually applies to those spirits of error themselves: they have eyes but do not see, nor do they acknowledge the image of God in Christ and in man; they have ears but do not hear, and a heart but understand nothing.

Furthermore, man is made in God's similitude and is like God in three ways: 
1. In the heavenly soul, which is eternal and immortal, and thus he is of God. 
2. Like him in power, being able to do much—namely according to his own portion, measure, and weight. 
3. Like him in glory, acting as a god and a lord and ruler over all other creatures.

Good reader, you must know and observe here that this great mystery is revealed, bringing with it the true <term>Let there be light</term> <note>original: "Jehi or" (יְהִי אוֹר); the Hebrew command from Genesis 1:3</note>—the day of the revelation of the Son of Man. Christ speaks of this expressly in Luke 17:22, etc., as the time when the Son of Man is revealed; and this is the revelation and ap-

There is but *one man in one person*, but in him are three witnesses of his substance—namely body, soul, and spirit—and seven powers. The spirit always stands in place of the Father, and is the Father himself also, and begets for himself a body through the soul (as the true mother). In this way, he who sees the Father also sees the Son: the Word itself, the life-giving <note>original: "quickening"</note> Spirit, and the life-giving *Adam* himself.

Furthermore, *God* begets a Son, not according to *person* or a personal distinction (as one man begets another man, which would mean there are two Gods, though there is only one *God*); rather, for our sakes, he begets a Son according to the testimony. This is so that we—by that witness of the Father and Son in *God*—might be made God's children, heirs, and co-heirs out of his seed, flesh, and bones. For *God*, in and for himself, needs neither Father nor Son, because there is never more than one in him; he himself is the root <note>original: "Iesse" (Jesse), referring to the "Root of Jesse" from Isaiah 11:1, a symbol of the messianic lineage</note> and all in all. There are not two or three, but *one* only, and no one else.

## CHAPTER 20.

### *Of the Truth and Spirit, by which all Wisdom is justified.*

When we intend to speak of *Wisdom*, it must be done in Spirit and *Truth*. Now, nothing is *Truth* except the *Spirit* alone; he can lead us into all *Truth*, teach us everything, and tell us of things to come. For all *spirits* are in *subjection* to him. He penetrates through them all, just as fire acts upon *gold* and *silver* seven times; and he does not undo the good that remains in it. Instead, he considers the blessing within it and breathes a fire of life, light, and *fire* into the "smoking flax" <note>A reference to Isaiah 42:3 and Matthew 12:20: "a smoldering wick he will not quench," meaning God sustains even the weakest faith.</note>, infusing it

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the purification process necessary to attain Wisdom, using the metaphor of refining gold in a furnace to describe the transformation of the human soul.</meta>

<page-num>72</page-num>

<header>Chapter 20. Of the Truth and Spirit,</header>

[it]self into the same, so that it may be fitted for a new creation, to be multiplied many thousands of times.

But nothing can attain *Wisdom* unless it first passes through the fire for a trial, just as *gold* cannot achieve its glory unless it has passed through the consuming fire in a *crucible* seven times. Afterward, it may be baptized with *water* and the *spirit* for a new birth and become a new kind of *gold*. From that same *spirit* and *water*, it increases many thousands of times, becoming a heavenly *gold*, *spirit*, and *metal*, through which other inferior metals may be transformed into the substance of the finest *gold*. So it is with the *man* who seeks *Wisdom*: first he must be baptized with *fire*, then with *water*, and then with the *spirit*. All of this is performed in the *crucible* of the *earthly man* <note>original: "Terrestrial man"</note>.

But all wisdom is sufficient through the *spirit* and in *truth*, through *principles, subordinate parts, and concordances* <note>original: "Principia, subordinata, & Concordantia"</note>, which later converge in a *harmony*. The *principles* contain the true beginning of every thing; everything returns to the place from which it came, and it is also preserved from that same source. The *subordinate parts* contain the *order*, directness, and perfection of every thing as they depend upon one another, standing and subsisting together. Just like a *ladder* or *stairs*, there must not be one step missing or out of place; otherwise, the *subordinate parts* are not true. Commonly there are seven *subordinate parts*, and they follow one another in an orderly way. Things that follow one after another are *subordinate*, and in this way, the system is perfect.

Lastly, this is a *concordance*: that all things may agree with one another, and no *conflict* <note>original: "Contrariety"</note> is found, seen, or heard anywhere. Just as in sweet *music* all things are harmonious, no matter how many *voices* there are, they change and flow into one another. An everlasting *triad* <note>original: "Ternarius"; likely referring to the Trinity or a threefold spiritual nature</note> remains within the music, and so the *principles* and *concordance* consist

At last the Harmony concentrates and encloses all, which takes everything together in *One, Three*, and *Seven*, and presents one as the other—namely the upper as the lower, and the lower as the upper <note>This reflects the Hermetic maxim "as above, so below," suggesting that the physical world reflects the spiritual world</note>; so that none be against the other. You are to *honor* him, and do not think, speak, or do anything against him, so that you may not be condemned out of your own mouth.

Where these three are in, on, and about *man*, there is really *Spirit, Truth*, and *Wisdom*. The *Divine Light* contains all *wisdom, understanding*, and *knowledge*. The *Divine Life* contains all *Truth*...

### Chapter 20. Of the Truth and Spirit

...itself into the same, so that it may be prepared for a new Creation, for a multiplication into many thousands.

But nothing may attain *Wisdom* unless it has first gone to the fire for a trial, just as *Gold* cannot reach its glory unless it has passed through the consuming fire seven times in the *Crucible* <note>A ceramic or metal container in which metals may be melted or subjected to very high temperatures; here used as a metaphor for spiritual testing</note>. Afterward, it may be baptized with *Water* and *Spirit* into a new birth and become a new *Gold*—increased into many thousands out of that same *Spirit* and *Water*. As a heavenly *Gold*, *Spirit*, and *Metal*, it can turn other inferior metals into the substance of the best *Gold*. So it is with the *man* who would gain *Wisdom*: first he must be baptized with *Fire*, then with *Water*, and then with the *Spirit*; and all this is done in the *Crucible* of the earthly <note>original: "Terrestrial"</note> *man*.

But all wisdom is sufficient through the *Spirit* and in *Truth*, through *Principles* <note>original: "Principia"</note>, *subordinates* <note>original: "subordinata"</note>, and *Concordance* <note>original: "Concordantia"</note>, which afterward center in a *Harmony*. The *Principles* contain the true beginning of everything: from where a thing came, it also returns, and from that source it is also preserved. The *subordinates* contain the *Order*, directness, and perfection of everything, as they hang together, stand, and exist by one another. Just like a *Ladder* or *Stairs*, there must not be one step missing or wrong, otherwise the *subordinates* are not true. Commonly there are seven *subordinates* which follow one after another in order; things that follow one after another are *subordinate*, and thus the system is perfect.

Lastly, this is a *Concordance*: that all things may agree with one another, and a *Contrariety* be found, seen, or heard nowhere. Just as in sweet *Music* all things are harmonious—no matter how many *Voices* there are, changing and flowing from one into another—an everlasting *Trio* <note>original: "Ternarius," referring to the number three or a group of three in a philosophical or alchemical context</note> remains within. In this way, the *Principles* and *Concordance* consist

# Chap. 20. Of the Truth and Spirit, &c.

Holiness and Righteousness: And the *Divine Love* contains the whole Spirit and pours him out into our hearts. Through this, we know that *God* has loved us, because he has given us his Spirit, which cries out in us *Abba* <note>Aramaic: a term for "Father" expressing an intimate, childlike relationship with God</note>, and gives testimony to our spirit that we are the *Children of God*. He pours forth our tears and prayers before *God* so that we might find grace before the *Lord*. He teaches us to pray correctly before the Lord about things that are above and makes intercession for us with unspeakable sighs <note>A reference to Romans 8:26, describing the Spirit’s help in prayer when words fail</note>.

Through this, we know in the Spirit and in Truth where the right *Wisdom*, the *Divine Truth*, and the *Holy Spirit* are. For these three—*Light, Life, and Love*—proceed from *God*. *God* himself is *Light*, and there is neither *darkness* nor *fire* <note>In this mystical context, "fire" likely refers to the "consuming fire" of judgment or wrath, which the author argues is absent from God’s pure essence</note> in or about him. *God* is *Eternal Life*; there is neither *death* nor *perdition* <note>original: "perdition," meaning spiritual ruin or eternal loss</note> apart from him, in him, or about him. *God* is *Love* itself, and there is neither *wrath*, nor *pain, hell* nor *damnation* apart from him. 

He who stays by, in, and on these three—namely by the *Divine Light*, in a *Divine Life*, and in a *Divine Love*—stays and abides in *God*, and *God* in him, in the Spirit and Truth. This is according to the wisdom and true knowledge of *God*. Such a person knows what Truth, Spirit, and Wisdom are and expresses their true principles, subordinates, and harmonies in a Divine Harmony, which is proven to the *Elect Angels* <note>Holy or chosen angels</note> and *men* in Spirit and in Truth.

# CHAP. XXI.

## *Of the Mystery of Time, to understand it aright.*

Nothing is so secret at night that the day may not reveal it, when the Light comes to its Day, and the day to its light, and the clear Sun shines over all that is under Heaven. The Night is past, and behold, the day breaks with its fair morning light, which is a light fire and a fire-light. Who can now survive? For the Lord comes—yes, the Lord is coming! Amen, Hallelujah! He is like the fire of a *Founder* <note>original: "Founder," a craftsman who melts and casts metal; here, God is compared to a refiner who purifies souls through the "fire" of judgment</note>, and like the sharp lye <note>original: "Lee," an alkaline solution used to make soap and clean cloth</note> of *soap-boilers*; he will melt, prove, and test. He will wash, purify, and cleanse, *And who can stand before him? (Malachi 3)*. He does this so that all filth may be cleared away before the Sun rises, so that the whole Earth and world may not be sentenced to banishment or destruction. *(Malachi 4)*.

Now that the day has come with its light in this time, the Mystery of the time of the whole world will be revealed. But it is always included and enclosed within the number of Seven. For on the seventh day God finished the Creation, and so it is always included in Seven. But the number 7 stands thus: 7, 49, 70. The number *Seven*, regarding our time, stands chiefly upon the *Seventh Trumpet*; in and with it, the *Mystery* of *GOD* is finished—indeed, revealed. *(Revelation 10, 11, 12)*. This is also the case with the seventh *Vial* of *God's* wrath. But as far as we know in part, we are currently living between the fifth and sixth *Vial*. The number 49 expressly shows that fair *Mystery* of the time of *refreshing* <note>In biblical numerology, 49 (7x7) often precedes the Jubilee year, a time of rest and restoration</note>

# Chapter 21. Of the Mystery of Time,

and the restoration of all that is lost (Leviticus 25). And the number 49 is the end of the "little seventh day" and the beginning of the "great seventh day"—the Sabbath of **God**. Finally, the number 70 points toward the seventy weeks mentioned by the Prophet **Daniel**, as well as the expiration of the 1,335 days (Daniel 9:24 and 12:12). When these periods are completed, then transgression will be reconciled, every prophecy fulfilled, and the most Holy One, together with his saints, will take the Kingdom. **Jerusalem** will be rebuilt, eternal righteousness established, and everything that has been lost by the Fall <note>The "Fall" refers to the original sin of Adam and Eve and their subsequent expulsion from Eden</note> shall be restored.

The Spirit prophesies of this great glory and unspeakable joy in all creatures—indeed, in all the lights of heaven and in all the elements. But where are the seers, where are the hearers, and where are the observers?

Furthermore, concerning the time of the world, it is divided into 1, 3, and 7. The "one time" generally contains the whole "great day" of the world, consisting of 12 hours (Matthew 12) <note>original: "Mat. 12," likely referring to the concept of the twelve hours of the day mentioned in John 11:9 or the laborers in Matthew 20</note>. This represents 6,000 years, meaning there are 500 years to every hour. However, the days shall be shortened, so that they will not reach a full 6,000 years; the days or years of that shortening are made clear in the Book of **Genesis**, during the first judgment over the world, etc. The "three times" of the world are now divided; specifically, the 6,000 years are split into three periods:

1.  The first period is from **Adam** until **Noah** at the Deluge <note>The Great Flood</note>, and it contains 1,656 years.
2.  The second period is from the Deluge until the **Messiah** was born of the **Virgin Mary**. This period is divided into 12 parts, each containing 214 years (or 214 and a half years), which added together make 2,563 years from the Deluge until the **Messiah** (4 Esdras 14:11) <note>original: "4 Esd. 14. 11." This refers to the apocryphal book of 2 Esdras in modern Bibles, which contains visions about the duration of the world</note>. Now, if 1,656 is added to the other, then the **Messiah** was born into the world of the **Virgin Mary** in the year of the world 4230.

The third time of the world begins from **Christ’s** nativi-

->**Calculation**<-

Concerning the previously mentioned threefold number (7, 49, 70), the mystery is clearly signified there without any subtraction or addition, if only you will open your eyes, ears, and hearts to see, hear, and observe. It could not possibly be told more clearly. These numbers themselves are calculated—namely, to know certainly how many years each of the twelve hours contains—because the 6,000 years are not complete, but those days must be shortened.

Just as in former times and judgments upon the world, seven days always passed before the judgment came (Genesis 7:4; Joshua 6; Leviticus 25). So it is now with the time of the judgment upon this world. Now, when the judgment is proclaimed, seven days go before the proclamation. If you have the spirit of **Daniel**, then calculate and reckon how many days have passed, and how many remain until the judgment. No one believes what changes are at hand; the whole world lies in wickedness, and it will perish in it.

But so that we may keep nothing from the reader and those who desire wisdom, and so that they may fully understand the time of the end—specifically through its certain signs—there are three signs of it. The first is that immediately after the great, horrible, bloody battle that is at hand, he whom we expect shall come (Malachi 3 & 4), and the Gospel of the Kingdom will be preached in the whole world as a testimony to all nations, so that there may be one Shepherd and one flock (Matthew 24:14; Revelation 14:6; Zephaniah 3:8–9). The second sign <note>original: "time"</note> is when the Ten Lost Tribes of **Israel** are found again across the water in the land, and they come upon Mount **Israel** from the East <note>original: "Orient"</note> after the sixth vial is poured out <note>In the Book of Revelation, the "sixth vial" (or bowl) of wrath refers to the drying up of the river Euphrates to prepare the way for the "kings of the East"</note>.

->**In Three Parts.**<-

---

In the *first* and *second parts* are contained such *chemical books* as have been written originally or translated into *English*, with a detailed account of their *titles*, various *editions*, and *volumes*.

->LIKEWISE.<-

In the *third part* is contained a collection of such things published in the *Philosophical Transactions* <note>The world's first scientific journal, established in 1665.</note> of the *Royal Society* (spanning *ten years*) that pertain to *chemistry*, or the study of *nature* by *art* <note>In this context, "art" refers to human skill, technology, or laboratory manipulation of natural materials.</note> in the *animal*, *vegetable*, and *mineral kingdoms*.

---

Collected by *William Cooper*, Bookseller, at the *Pelican* <note>Before street numbers, businesses were identified by signs; Cooper’s shop was marked by a Pelican.</note> in *Little Britain*, *London*.

---

->*London*, printed in the year 1675.<-

<summary>This is the title page of William Cooper’s 1675 bibliography, which catalogs English-language chemical and alchemical texts and relevant articles from the Royal Society's Philosophical Transactions.</summary>
